## Direct everyday lookups and initial public source — October 21, 2026

Nine common workspace reads now have immediate schemas in voice and local Quick requests: saved methods, assistants, groups, routines, files, spending, mail search/read and calendar. Other capabilities remain discoverable. No read runs until the model requests it. Saved pre-upgrade discovery steps can still load promoted read schemas without executing them. Stored-mail search matches all keywords across sender, subject and body, with quoted phrases; connected Google search keeps provider syntax. Finance tool results expose formatted USD amounts, while accounting and result cards retain integer cents.

The full automated suite passes **1,267 tests**, with zero failures, cancellations or skips. All 49 focused cases and the production build pass. Tests cover direct typed and voice reads, restart replay, disabled tools, discovery compatibility, stored/connected mail behavior and exact currency values.

Actual Qwen3.5:9b Quick acceptance after the currency fix passes **five of six** fictional scenarios: exact saved-method names, spending ($42.84), specialists, stored mail and reminders. The calendar case returned a statement about checking the calendar without executing `list_calendar`; it fails acceptance. Earlier receipts retain a 9B cents-as-dollars error, a 27B initial timeout and an overly strict numeric test that rejected a correct amount followed by sentence punctuation. The parser was corrected and checked against eight positive/negative examples; no earlier receipt was overwritten. Source receipts are local, excluded from the public repository, and are not evidence that every provider passes.

These changes add no user steps. General tool-use reliability, natural voice latency, physical audio/device coverage and signed distribution remain open. The prior packaged-app and voice results below apply to their recorded builds; they are not presented as a fresh native certification of this source revision.

The public source is prepared from the explicit release allowlist with a fresh Git history. Owner workspaces, credentials, copied reference products, generated application packages and private acceptance captures are excluded. Native fixtures use the current home directory instead of a developer-specific path.

## Tool discovery must lead to actual work — October 21, 2026

The compact tool directory now groups exact names and preserves short purpose hints, including access to stored mail/calendar while a live account is disconnected. Full schemas and permissions are unchanged. Imported spending results explicitly identify USD integer cents; their displayed summary is unchanged. Metadata-only discovery cannot by itself establish that a lookup was completed. Relay withholds that draft, requests one continuation and reports failure if the model still does not act. The boundary survives restart and respects cancellation and owner steering. Repeated discovery that exhausts the run fails without offering an unusable retry; earlier result cards remain available.

The final automated suite passes **1,261 tests**, with no failures or skips. This includes twelve added cases: two directory/currency checks and ten completion-boundary checks. The focused run passes 43 tests. The production build passes. A preceding full run under heavy shared-host load had six failures in existing DNS/request timing, PDF/email extraction deadlines and server startup/restart; its log is retained. With local inference stopped, the complete unchanged regression set passes in 190.757 seconds. No deadlines or assertions were relaxed.

Actual-model lookup acceptance is still incomplete. The six fictional scenarios cover methods, spending, calendar, specialist assistants, synced mail and reminders. The earlier guarded 9B run passed four: a metadata-only specialist answer was correctly blocked, while an overly narrow mail search stopped early. The final clarified 27B run also passed four. It actually read the saved method but omitted the numeric portion of its requested exact name, and it asked an unnecessary question without searching stored mail. Its reminder scenario recovered from a metadata-only answer, performed the actual read and returned the correct paused reminder in four model requests. Saved-data and approval state remained unchanged. These failures remain in the receipts; a complete lookup pass is not claimed.

The earlier 27B attempt under host load passed two cases, including one initial model timeout. Before the completion guard, the paired 9B directory trials passed two of six with the old directory and three with the grouped directory. These small, variable runs are diagnostic evidence, not statistical model comparisons or speed benchmarks. The grouped directory keeps every exact name and full deferred schema while reducing instruction text; no general latency improvement is claimed.

The actual Whisper Base / Qwen3.5:27b Quick / MCP / Mac speech journey passes with three selected sources, exactly two remote app reads, two initial model requests and one correction request. The corrected answer keeps all five facts and uses exactly two sentences. The unchanged packaged Mac renderer reopens it at phone width and preserves the unfinished draft against the current disposable backend. Input audio is synthetic; recognition, inference, app reads and speech generation are real. Initial utterance-end to audio was 149.628 seconds, and correction to audio was 26.695 seconds. This is not acceptable natural conversation latency, and variable shared-host load prevents a speed claim.

Source and packaged-runtime identity, mobile web-byte reuse, native Mac startup/restart checks and owner-data preservation are indexed in `../relay-evidence/tool-directory-verification.json` when present and matching this source tree. That record separates passing implementation checks from incomplete live lookup acceptance. Previous failure receipts and the failed full-suite log remain available.

The completion check is deliberately bounded: it covers a model stopping after loading tool instructions, not all tool-free initial answers, poor searches, or unsupported conclusions after a real operation. It adds no user step or popup. The broader JARVIS goal remains active.

## Selected documents in one read step — October 21, 2026

Relay can now read up to eight selected sources together: readable attachments and automatic fixed app documents. Reads retain their existing permissions, operation receipts and source identities. The whole selection is validated before dispatch; individual results are checked again after all reads settle. Cancellation, changed files or revoked app access discard affected content. Large results explicitly require an individual follow-up read. App templates and reviewed actions keep their existing paths. This introduces no user controls or setup steps.

The final automated suite passes 1,249 tests with no failures or skips, including ten new selected-source cases and both typed and spoken durable replies. A focused run of 38 source, voice and conversation checks also passes after the correction guidance changed. The production build passes. Tests cover validation before reads, parallel dispatch, source provenance, idempotent app receipts, per-source errors, truncation, cancellation, late content changes and revoked access.

The real Whisper Base / Qwen3.5:27b Quick / MCP / Mac-voice journey passes with three selected sources, exactly two remote document reads, two model requests for the first answer and one for its correction. The correction retains the meeting time, room, code and cost, changes book to sketchbook, and obeys the requested two sentences. The packaged Mac renderer reopens the saved result at phone width without losing the draft or creating duplicate turns. The microphone input is synthetic; recognition, inference, app reads and speech generation are real.

The smaller 9B model also chose the combined read, but initially lost facts during correction. Revised voice guidance retained the facts while still missing the requested sentence count. A factual-only passing receipt therefore did not establish full instruction following; the harness now checks the two-sentence requirement explicitly. All three 9B attempts remain available, including the stricter failure. The 27B result is one bounded acceptance scenario, not a guarantee across models or requests.

The strict 27B run took 77.226 seconds from the initial utterance ending to first audio, and 11.946 seconds for the correction. These shared-host observations do not establish a speed improvement. Fewer model round trips and parallel source reads are verified; natural voice latency remains open. The current production web files still match all 48 files in the prior verified iOS and Android packages. Those native journeys were not repeated for this backend change. Physical audio, retail devices, fresh dependency installation and signed distribution remain outside this increment.

The aggregate source, runtime, test and owner-data checks are indexed in `../relay-evidence/selected-source-verification.json` when present and matching this source tree. The broader JARVIS goal remains active.

## Speech starts before the whole recording is ready — October 21, 2026

The Mac speech adapter now delivers bounded PCM from its own growing temporary WAV file. The voice call schedules those samples immediately after receiving enough audio to begin. It still waits for a completed assistant run before generating speech. Interruption cancels generation and every scheduled source; a failed tail stops playback and allows retry of the completed answer without another model request. Only a complete recording is persisted, with its original WAV bytes and one compact memo.

The full automated suite passes 1,239 tests, with no failures or skips. The production build passes. After tightening the streaming source-role guard, all 34 focused speech/call/streaming tests pass again. Those tests include exact signed PCM decoding, sample order, no partial storage, source changes, incomplete transport and authenticated interruption. The existing external-provider fallback passes twelve browser checks. New gated playback checks pass six desktop/phone browser conditions and three packaged Mac conditions, including a failure after audio has started and a successful retry without repeating work.

The packaged Mac test with the actual installed voice receives first PCM at 6.484 seconds and completes synthesis at 12.434 seconds. A separate actual Whisper Base / Qwen3.5:9b Quick / app-document / Mac-voice journey passes both spoken turns. Their first PCM arrives at 2.822 and 2.813 seconds into synthesis; the complete recordings take 6.537 and 7.181 seconds. Both stages are measured in the same synthesis operation, so the removed full-recording wait is observable without comparing different generated replies. The full end-of-utterance-to-audio times are still 32.587 and 8.432 seconds. These are functional observations on a shared host, not controlled speed benchmarks, physical microphone measurements or broad certification of the smaller model.

One iPhone Simulator XCTest passes twelve conditions with incremental PCM and thirteen native captures. The Android debug APK passes nine checks with incremental PCM, native captures, compact replay, interruption, recovery and navigation. These mobile runs use synthetic microphone input and controlled services. The actual speech and inference run is separate and uses the packaged Mac app.

Failures are retained. The first live-model attempt timed out taking its initial screenshot before any spoken turn; the test had explicitly hidden its window. The rerun keeps its test window visible without focusing it. Android's first attempt failed to locate the Connect control by resource id; its second failed with a case-insensitive regex. A native accessibility probe observed the enabled `CONNECT` button, confirmed exact string/resource matches, and showed that the regex flag was not honored. The final run uses the exact visible label and passes with the original deadlines. The diagnostic process was stopped after it retained a driver handle.

Receipts, source/package hashes, retained failures and cleanup are indexed in `../relay-evidence/voice-streaming-verification.json` when present and matching the source tree. Source and mobile archives are immutable outputs under `release/`; no source installation from a fresh dependency cache was repeated. The owner preview remains sample/unconfigured with its nine messages and two files preserved. The broader JARVIS goal remains active: faster inference, physical-device acoustics and release signing remain separate work.

## A simpler voice history — October 21, 2026

Playback now also stops when the layout is hidden by Suspense. A deliberately held destination-page bundle verifies this before loading is released; returning to chat does not restart audio. ## Recovery after a stopped private computer — October 19, 2026

The assistant now reconciles confirmed computer-action receipts when a new task checkpoint was interrupted during a command, desktop input, ordinary file write or directory creation. A host-only lookup requires the same operation, request scope and exact arguments; no guest call occurs during receipt recovery. Later calls from the old plan are discarded, old images are removed and owner corrections apply after retaining the completed result. Missing, interrupted and mismatched receipts stop without automatic replay; legacy checkpoints and combined file transformations/attachments keep their existing conservative behavior.

Nineteen new regression cases cover SQLite reopen, completed commands/writes/directories/clicks, stale plans and images, missing/uncertain/mismatched/cross-request receipts, old checkpoints, changed owner instructions, cancellation, ownership, lease loss, disabled computers, unsupported tools and a model connection failing after recovery. The focused suite initially hit a sandbox denial when opening a localhost HTTP test server; it was rerun with local-server permission. This was an environment failure, not a passing test.

The actual ARM64 VM acceptance kills a real worker process and force-stops the dedicated VM at both confirmed and uncertain action boundaries. Confirmed work returns the exact retained Recovery.txt to the original chat once. An uncertain command's fsynced first effect survives, its later effect is absent, and the restarted worker reports failure without another model call or guest action. The script verifies ownership before interruption, no owner-folder mounts, preservation of the selected Docker context and unrelated VM status, and complete fixture cleanup. These tests use controlled model responses and do not establish autonomous model judgment, physical host power-loss recovery, recovery of unsaved application memory, or interrupted software-update recovery.

The current verification index is `../relay-evidence/computer-interruption-verification.json` when present and matching the source tree. No user interface or mobile web assets changed. The broader JARVIS goal remains active.

## Explicit reminder completion and preserved follow-up answers — October 19, 2026

`create_reminder` now accepts an optional `finish` choice. A true choice can complete a single fixed-text reminder immediately after its verified save, using current host state for the confirmation. The final checkpoint is saved before that confirmation is emitted. Pending batch actions, failed or removed saves, missing checked deliverables, stop, in-flight corrections, selected methods and selected apps prevent this shortcut. Prose accompanying any tool step also preserves the model continuation, even if the model incorrectly marks its reminder call complete. This avoids dropping an additional answer such as an arithmetic result that arrived beside a tool call.

An explicit false choice preserves the remaining model answer and survives checkpoint restart. The next attempt receives the saved reminder's current status outside the shared system prefix. Before creation, temporary result guidance identifies the current owner request and distinguishes earlier conversation from pending work. These are bounded host checks and current-task guidance, not a general proof that a model understood every part of a request. Omitted choices retain legacy behavior when no explanatory tool prose is present; model judgment can still miss an unexpressed additional answer. No new owner controls or confirmation steps are added.

Seventeen new controlled tests cover a one-step save and speech callback, durable replay, false/omitted choices, remaining batch actions, a missing file, invalid and removed saves, a paused or already-delivered reminder, stop, correction, malformed flags, restricted routine work, checkpoint continuation, stable system instructions, preservation of current/earlier tool prose and selected-method continuation. The existing result-context suite adds a reminder case. Eighty focused tests pass before release verification.

Development failures are retained under `../relay-evidence/reminder-fast-finish`: `live-27b` chose a longer path and answered a Chinese request in English; `live-27b-checked` recovered from an invented tool argument but failed an overly strict one-call expectation; `live-27b-release` replayed earlier reminder requests during recovery and omitted the current arithmetic answer; `live-27b-current-request` saved just the right reminder but its short confirmation discarded an answer provided beside the tool call. Those failures motivated current-request guidance and the explanation-preservation boundary. `live-27b-complete` retained normal continuation but failed an exact-confirmation wording assertion. Functional acceptance now checks current-state wording separately from measured model call counts, since longer continuation is valid. `live-27b-final` passed the reminder and arithmetic cases, then failed the exact file bytes because its unquoted final period was omitted. The final file prompt explicitly quotes the same required payload; the byte assertion is unchanged.

The final five-case harness uses actual Qwen 3.5 27B Quick through the browser composer, worker and SQLite store, on isolated port 41334. It checks one saved result for each owner request, exact relative deadlines, the paused daily schedule, Chinese confirmation, the additional arithmetic answer, and an attached plain-text file with exact contents. It records response tool calls and elapsed times, reloads at phone width and cleans up the isolated model and data. This is typed browser acceptance, not microphone, native-phone or real-account acceptance. Individual timings are observations under changing host conditions, not a controlled speed benchmark or proof of natural voice latency.

The final run, retained failures, inspected captures, full clean-source rebuild, packaged Mac checks, unchanged mobile package hashes and owner-preview preservation are indexed in `../relay-evidence/reminder-fast-finish-verification.json` when that receipt is present and matches the source tree. The complete JARVIS goal, general model reliability, other languages, physical audio, native distribution and broader cross-app acceptance remain open.

## Current-result guidance and fewer repeated questions — October 19, 2026

Pending plain-text file, saved-skill and email-review instructions are prepared after conversation recall for each model attempt, using the current host result checks. They guide the first action and disappear after verified delivery. This temporary guidance is neither persisted into chat/checkpoints nor inserted into the shared system prefix. In particular, saving an email no longer changes that prefix. Required-question routing still runs first, and a bound filename answer receives continuation guidance instead of another ask-first instruction. Tool availability, action reviews, completion fingerprints and repair budgets remain unchanged.

Actual-model testing found two interaction failures. The first run rejected “What would you like to name the file? (It will be saved with a .txt extension.)” because the future-tense hint contained “saved.” The existing narrow recovery now discards future-tense hints while still rejecting completion claims in the question or elsewhere. The next run repeated the filename question after correctly binding the spoken answer. An exact repeated `ask_question` prompt now returns an internal error with the saved answer, without posting another question. A distinct clarification remains available, and later corrections release the check. Case, whitespace and a trailing question mark are ignored; this is not semantic deduplication of every possible rephrasing.

Twelve focused transport/result tests cover all three output types, an unchanged system prefix, status disappearing after a real save, failed writes, restart without duplication, removal before restart, ordinary conversation, pending-question routing, in-flight corrections, recovery from an answered question repeated by the model, and normal question validation without a bound run. A final code review found a missing null check in that last case; its regression was reproduced and fixed. The existing durable prose-question test now uses the observed future-tense hint. During test development, two fixture mistakes were corrected: an empty filename is a valid defaulted write, so rejection uses an invalid text value; an immediate correction needs `delivery: "now"` to exercise steering.

The retained `result-context/live-27b` and `live-27b-checked` receipts record those two real failures. `live-27b-final` stopped before its first check because Ollama returned HTTP 500 for malformed generated tool-call XML. No action ran; cleanup completed, and the unchanged build was retried. These attempts are not counted as successful acceptance.

The complete `live-27b-release` Mac run passes all five checks with actual Qwen 3.5 27B Quick, Whisper Base and system speech. The spoken filename continues the saved request, writes one exact file and speaks its result without another question or a false-completion repair. This uses three model requests for the spoken continuation and nine overall, versus four and ten in the preceding verified run. It takes 35.0 seconds from utterance end to audio, versus the preceding 33.0 seconds; recognition takes 2.1 seconds and synthesis 2.0 seconds. Fewer steps did not establish faster speech. Email continuation takes 28.3 seconds, and its system prefix remains identical before and after the review is saved. These are individual observations under varying host conditions, not controlled benchmarks. All four captures were inspected: the phone-width reload shows the saved file, voice memo and retained draft without horizontal overflow. The email reply still incorrectly describes the following card as “above” and suggests editing there; that model-generated guidance remains a quality gap.

The separate `typed-27b-checked` Mac journey passes Word creation, an exact same-document replacement, a paused daily reminder, an unsent email review and a saved reusable method with the exact supplied instructions. Both DOCX files are retained and checked; saving the method does not execute it. Observed times are 32.4, 57.4, 27.3, 26.3 and 47.4 seconds. This verifies the capability flow, not natural conversation speed. The inspected cards are readable, while confirmations still repeat details and misstate the email card's position.

Final actual-model outcomes, inspected captures, clean-source checks, packaged-app/runtime equality, unchanged phone package hashes and owner-preview preservation are indexed in `../relay-evidence/result-context-verification.json`. This increment changes server behavior and adds regression coverage; it does not add native-phone or physical-microphone acceptance. General model reliability, response concision, natural voice latency and the full JARVIS scope remain open.

The actual-model receipts predate only the final missing-run null check. The aggregate verifies that exact one-line source difference and equality of every other recorded runtime source; the final clean-source suite covers both bound and unbound question calls. The native packaged-app check uses the final build.

## Compact Quick requests and verified text-file completion — October 19, 2026

Local Ollama Quick typed requests now use the same complete capability catalog as voice. Common schemas remain ready; explicit file/document/reminder/review requests preload the needed instructions. Other available tools load on demand. The chosen mode and loaded schemas survive restart and model fallback, while older typed checkpoints preserve their original full tool set. No additional owner control is introduced.

A pending question now uses a dedicated internal decision prompt identifying the actual owner message. The model either resumes that question or handles a separate request. An ordinary unrelated answer can finish inside the new-request decision. Required saved work cannot use that shortcut. Direct prose without a decision stays hidden for one recovery attempt, then fails explicitly. This avoids conflicting general-chat instructions during routing; it does not guarantee a model's intent classification.

Direct plain-text file requests now require a saved result with host-created provenance, current-request identity and a matching name/type/bytes fingerprint. A verified computer export or a completed, bound specialist result can also satisfy delivery. Old uploads, unrelated results and completion prose cannot. False completion text is withheld from streaming and speech, with one repair attempt and a durable limit. Successful writes survive disconnect without duplication. This checks presence and provenance, not arbitrary content correctness; the actual-model acceptance compares exact requested bytes separately. Ordinary saved notes remain free to use other formats.

All development attempts remain under `../relay-evidence/compact-quick`. An early 27B success used writing in the core schema, but a 9B run then created an unsolicited text file while preparing email. Writing is now preloaded only for an explicit text-file or saved-note request. The next 9B run claimed an email review without binding the question; a 27B run bound a filename answer but claimed a file without creating it. These motivated the routing and saved-result checks. Initial enforcement then exposed a skipped decision and an incorrect topic binding; the dedicated decision prompt separates that step from ordinary work. The final 9B trial still incorrectly bound a separate topic and failed email preparation. It remains failed acceptance, not certification of the faster model.

The complete `live-27b-decision` journey passes five checks with actual Qwen 3.5 27B Quick, the Mac renderer, Whisper Base and system speech: a required recipient question, separate arithmetic topic, exact typed-answer email review with its original attachment, required filename question, and spoken continuation that creates one exact file. During the last step the model actually fabricated completion; the host withheld it, recovered once and verified a real write before publishing or speaking. Ten total model requests were used, four for the spoken continuation. The initial tool definitions contain 10,015 characters, and routing uses two tools containing 1,426 characters. Recognition takes 9.6 seconds and audio starts 62.1 seconds after utterance end. No retained-audio retry, model HTTP error or renderer error occurred. These are individual observations, not a controlled speed comparison or natural conversational latency.

The first typed capability journey (`typed-27b`) passed the Word creation, exact revision, paused reminder and email checks, then falsely claimed to save a reusable skill. No skill existed. Direct saved-skill requests now require a host-bound, fingerprinted saved method before completion. Its schema remains deferred; one repair can load it and perform the save. The guard preserves version checks, successful saves through restart and completed specialist returns, and never executes the method. Eight focused tests cover scope, provenance, exact instructions, discovery, repair limits, versioning, questions and handoffs. An additional upgrade case preserves completed file and skill writes in older checkpoints without adding an output or version; new requests keep verification through restart. The earlier clean-source receipt (`verification-bfeee3ec6fa51410`) passed 1,173 tests but predates this live-discovered fix; it is retained as intermediate evidence.

The phone-width reload retains the result and independent composer draft. Desktop and phone-width captures were inspected. File delivery is clear; the email confirmation still repeats the review details and incorrectly calls the following card “above.” That wording remains a usability gap. The phone capture is browser layout evidence, not a new native-phone or physical-device test.

`scripts/test-voice-catalog-live.mjs --typed --mac` adds a separate actual-model composer journey for Word creation, an exact same-document replacement, a paused daily reminder, one unsent email review and saving a deferred reusable skill with exact instructions. Its receipt, saved DOCX files and captures remain alongside the speech journey. The aggregate `../relay-evidence/compact-quick-verification.json` records the final outcomes, clean-source test/build/reproduction checks, package/runtime equality, inspected captures, retained phone package hashes and preservation of the owner preview. Native phone sources and packages are unchanged in this increment. Physical acoustics, signed-device distribution, general model reliability, response concision and the complete JARVIS milestone remain open.

## Conversational answers to saved questions — October 19, 2026

A normal composer message or voice turn can answer a saved question and continue its original request without another message or run. The server binds the literal owner text and preserves the original assistant, attachments, app selections, skill, previous answers and restrictions. The context binding and deletion of the old planning checkpoint share one SQLite transaction. Focused regressions cover restart on either side of that transaction, changed accounts, stale questions, chat/assistant boundaries, quoted targets, group broadcasts, stop controls, tool-batch rejection and retention of completed work.

Pending questions now impose a real action boundary. A model that attempts ordinary tools before deciding whether this is an answer executes none of that batch. Its next step offers only answer/new-request routing. A subsequent prose completion claim fails explicitly, and undecided response text is held rather than streamed as progress. Conversational topic changes still answer directly. Explicit single-recipient group replies may continue that assistant's question; broadcasts and automatic routing cannot claim it.

Live testing found that stronger question instructions alone did not reliably make the 27B model save a filename question. A limited fallback now preserves a short prose question for an initial direct English work request that explicitly asks to ask before proceeding or to ask and wait. Quoted instructions, ordinary question-writing requests, generated work, completed tool work, secrets and approval prompts are excluded. Other missing-information questions still depend on normal model tool use. See FOLLOW_UPS.md for exact scope.

The real local-model Mac acceptance asks for a recipient, changes the topic to arithmetic, answers through the ordinary composer, and checks one exact unsent email review with the original attachment bytes. It then requests a named text file, supplies its name through synthetic microphone audio, and checks the original task, actual saved bytes, speech output and independent typed draft. It uses Whisper Base and Mac speech without owner accounts. Failed attempts remain under `../relay-evidence/conversational-answers`: a raw-Markdown renderer assertion, prose-only filename questions, a local recognition timeout, a 9B model that bypassed question resolution, and the same smaller model failing honestly after enforcement. A separate 27B voice attempt bound its recognized answer but heard “Blue Comment” for “Blue Comet” and then received HTTP 500 from the model service. Those failures are not successful voice acceptance. Later receipts also retain a renderer-observation failure with no proven cause and a test-input mistake: the harness expected “Meeting Notes” while its recording still said “Blue Comet.” The recording and assertions now derive from one value, and the receipt stores the spoken input and audio hash. The corrected focused journey passes (`voice-matched-input`): “Please name the file Meeting Notes” is recognized correctly, creates one exact text file, plays the spoken result and preserves the independent draft through reload. Its measured time from speech end to audio is 67.1 seconds; this is functional evidence, not natural conversation speed.

The complete final journey (`live-complete`) passes all five checks with Qwen 3.5 27B: saved recipient question, separate-topic preservation, exact typed-answer review, saved filename question and one spoken continuation. Recognition takes 2.1 seconds; speech begins 78.2 seconds after the utterance ends. No audio retry, model HTTP error or renderer error occurs. The phone-width reload retains the result and typed draft. Screenshots were inspected; the email confirmation duplicates some review details and remains more verbose than desired. The result is functionally verified, while latency and reply concision remain open usability work.

The first clean-source run (`verification-ea9daa7589652974`) retained 1,150 passing tests and one reproducible test-clock failure. The background-ideas test froze only its service at October 11 while storage used the real date, so it pruned the seven-day-old fixture notification. The test now advances one mocked Date clock for both layers; production expiry behavior is unchanged.

The final receipt and source/package checks are indexed by `../relay-evidence/conversational-answers-verification.json`. The harness records any use of the existing retained-audio Retry control and includes it in observed latency. This increment does not certify physical microphones, noisy speech, proper names, general model reliability or new native-phone behavior. Existing native-phone packages and the broader JARVIS acceptance milestones remain separate evidence and open gates.

## Native clarification recovery — October 18, 2026

Unfinished question answers now survive separate-page navigation, reload and native app termination without replacing the main composer draft. The recovered iPhone field resumes at the end on first focus; later selection remains editable. Answer or dismissal clears the displayed card's local copy, and failed submission retains it. This is device-local recovery, not cross-device synchronization of unsent answers.

The original controlled HTTP fixture uses the actual agent, worker, question commands and SQLite. It asks for a missing recipient, waits through partial native input and relaunch, then prepares one pending review with the owner's exact subject and multiline body. Native iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Simulator and Android 16 Google APIs emulator acceptance passed. Android additionally checks five reloads, two distinct process relaunches, accessibility, viewport fit and no recorded native crashes. No owner account or external send is used.

Receipts, screenshots and failed attempts are retained under `../relay-evidence/native-email-clarification`; `../relay-evidence/native-email-clarification-verification.json` identifies the final source and packaged builds. The initial Android System UI ANR and test-driver input/reconnection failures remain recorded; the driver now preserves letter case and attaches to the new process after relaunch. ## Focused voice instructions and verified document revisions — October 18, 2026

Detailed voice guidance now follows the schemas exposed for the current work. Loading skills, PDF, artifact or computer tools restores their full relevant instructions; universal source, permission, memory and completion rules remain present. A synthetic generic voice prompt falls from 7,120 to 4,712 characters. The full typed-chat system prompt is byte-identical to its saved baseline. Explicit creation requests expose the needed schema immediately, without executing anything early. This prompt reduction does not establish a latency improvement.

Actual-model tests found two separate failures. A 9B Quick request omitted the routine execution mode, silently producing an assistant task instead of a reminder. The model-facing schema now requires that choice and the runtime rejects omission before saving. A 27B request confused a delivered file ID with its editable result ID. `read_artifact` now accepts the exact file ID, resolves its bound version and returns the canonical revision target; stale edits and conflicting bindings remain rejected. A later 9B run read a document and claimed an update without saving. Direct, single-result “update that same Word document” requests now withhold completion until a new version of that same result is tied to the current request. One repair attempt remains available. Quoted/advice/example requests, attachments, selected-app requests, ambiguous preceding results and other chats do not infer this target. This is a conservative identity/provenance check, not a general judge of edit meaning. A completed, host-bound specialist handoff may satisfy it only when its returned file belongs to a newer version of the same result; unrelated, unchanged or mismatched files remain rejected. The final review added this handoff compatibility check after the live runs, whose scenarios do not delegate work.

The actual Qwen 3.5 9B default-reasoning run in `voice-guidance/capabilities-9b-reasoning` passes four cases: a real Word file, an exact same-document revision, a paused daily reminder and an unsent email review. Both exported Word files are checked, and the second document XML differs only by Charging cable → USB-C charger. Reminder title, exact text, execution mode, 09:00 Asia/Shanghai schedule and paused state match; email recipient, subject and body match and remain pending. All four headless Chrome captures were inspected. These inputs represent recognized speech, with no microphone or audio playback exercised in this capability test. Confirmations are understandable but still more verbose than necessary.

The evidence directory retains all earlier trials. The first guidance-only 27B Mac pass predates schema preloading and revision guards. Smaller Quick runs failed to save a requested file, selected the wrong reminder mode, failed to save a revision, or never created the reminder; these are not counted as passes. One new assertion initially looked for the title on an artifact rather than its version and was corrected. Native screenshot timeouts occurred at both 15 and 60 seconds; the capture timeout now applies only to test screenshots, and semantic result checks are recorded before capturing. A headless 27B run then exceeded the unchanged two-minute model deadline during severe host memory pressure. Final successful capability observations are 62.3, 63.6, 68.6 and 37.9 seconds, respectively. They are not natural conversation latency, a controlled benchmark, or a new Mac UI acceptance result. Owner processes and credentials were not used to resolve these failures.

The speech/private-computer trial in `voice-guidance/computer-9b-reasoning` uses real Whisper Base, Qwen 9B default reasoning, connected synthetic documents, private Linux file operations and Mac speech through a headless browser. It completed an in-flight correction with two distinct writes, no repeated operation, and exact corrected export bytes. It then failed at a case-sensitive preview assertion: the actual heading/list used “Sketchbook,” while the harness required lowercase “sketchbook.” The preview check now accepts capitalization while the exact downloaded-byte comparison and all required facts remain unchanged. This failed receipt is retained; the complete rerun under `computer-9b-checked` passes. It verifies one run with two spoken turns, two distinct writes, the corrected room/code/noon/sketchbook/$33 facts, identical private-computer/export/download bytes, draft retention, capture shutdown and a separate phone-width file page. The observed total is 125.9 seconds and correction-to-audio 36.2 seconds; the earlier partial trial took 386.8 and 137.2 seconds. Variable host load and output prevent a speed claim. Desktop/phone-width captures were inspected. The spoken response still describes an export as “Downloaded as” and includes an unnecessary byte count; that wording remains a quality gap.

`voice-guidance-verification.json` records the clean-source result, package/runtime equality, live receipts, inspected captures, owner-preview preservation and test-resource cleanup for this increment. The source suite includes seven added cases plus expanded saved-file/version assertions. Mobile web sources are unchanged and this increment adds no native-phone or physical-microphone acceptance. Faster natural voice, broader model reliability, signed physical-device distribution and the full JARVIS milestones remain open.

Voice now starts with a compact catalog and the complete schemas for common conversation, selected documents and computer file tools. Commands, file writes and mkdir can use durable host-owned operation IDs, while explicit retries still enforce exact arguments. This adds no owner interaction.

A separate real-model capability journey found a false completion: the model said a reminder was saved without creating it. Direct routine/reminder creation requests now withhold completion text until a real current-request routine exists. One repair attempt asks the model to perform the missing work; repeated unsupported claims fail explicitly. Provenance is committed with the routine by the host. Old, unrelated, archived routines and navigation cards do not satisfy the check. Genuine missing-information questions still work, and saved work survives model disconnects. The Word document XML differs only by the requested Charging cable → USB-C charger replacement. The reminder has the requested text, daily 09:00 Asia/Shanghai schedule and paused state; the email remains unsent. This capability journey uses synthetic recognized-text inputs, not audio capture. The earlier failure is retained. Screenshot review confirms readable result/source cards and the review controls; generated replies still inaccurately refer to cards as “above” and could be shorter.

The initial clean-source run timed out during two local connector setups under concurrent load. One setup registered cleanup too late and left its fixture listening; the failed process was stopped after inspection and the failed report remains in `verification-f8776614e35364bc`. Cleanup now registers before discovery, and the full suite runs one test file at a time with unchanged assertions and connector deadlines. A second loaded-host run (`verification-9614d0cec27db923`) terminated normally but exposed connector/Office/PDF timeouts and a backup-expiry test that checked the filesystem before asynchronous removal finished. That test now advances a controlled timer through its exact deadline and awaits the real removal promise; production timeouts remain unchanged. The complete suite passes 1,051 tests. `voice-catalog-verification.json` indexes source/package equality, the speech journey, the four capability cases, failures, visual review and cleanup. Native phone sources and packages are unchanged. Physical acoustics, native-device acceptance for new flows, wider model/task reliability and the full JARVIS milestones remain open.

## Document recovery without another setup step — October 15, 2026

Recognized transient failures in HTTP MCP document reads now get one automatic retry within the original timeout budget. Partial JSON/SSE results are discarded, the failed session closes, and a new session rechecks access, revision, arguments and live metadata. One successful receipt saves one result; a failed receipt prevents redispatch when that operation resumes. Generic tools, prompt retrieval, local stdio, cancellation, metadata/access changes, authentication, protocol and size errors do not inherit retry behavior. The model receives a distinct unavailable-document result rather than an uncertain external-action result.

The first actual-model regression exposed a separate failure: the 27B model omitted the requested date/tag and retrieved a default resource path. That run remains under `document-recovery/app-live`. Agent invocation now rejects empty path-selection fields before dispatch and gives a schema-recovery instruction. Optional query/fragment expansions remain optional, explicit owner forms retain RFC 6570 semantics, and saved connection fingerprints stay unchanged. The fixture now returns the unpredictable meeting room only for the exact requested date/tag, so a plausible answer from a default note cannot pass.

The actual Qwen 3.5 27B Quick Mac journey passes seven cases: combined text/CSV, exact dated/tagged note, hostile source instructions, changed definition rejected before reading, interrupted SSE reply recovered, persistent unavailability stopped after two reads, and a chosen planning template with its Main draft retained. The recovered response answers normally, with no reconnect/setup instruction or extra owner interaction. The unavailable response identifies missing data without inventing a code or recommending reconnection. Changed metadata still requires its existing Refresh step. These use independently authored synthetic app data, not live owner accounts. Answer text and screenshots are reviewed; the planning response can still be more verbose than necessary.

Twenty-two added test cases/subcases cover interrupted and partial JSON/SSE reads, exact URI reuse, one saved file, durable successful/failed receipts, bounded time, changed metadata, revocation, cancellation, authentication including a broken response body, protocol errors, size limits, tool/prompt no-replay and missing path fields. The complete suite contains 1,037 tests. `document-recovery-verification.json` indexes the clean-source build, package/runtime equality, actual-model receipts, diagnostic failure, owner-state preservation and cleanup. The frontend and mobile packages are unchanged; this increment makes no new native-phone, physical-audio or voice-latency claim. Natural voice speed, broader model quality and the full JARVIS milestones remain open.

Selected fixed resources now expose up to eight direct tools that take no model-generated fields. Their stable names bind the selected connection ID/revision and exact resource entry. Old aliases fail after a selection/revision change; arbitrary fields are rejected. Parameterized documents and prompt templates retain their existing flow, and all ordinary tools remain advertised. Catalogs beyond the existing bounds use ordinary discovery.

Four added regression cases cover fixed-resource-only exposure, unchanged base tools, exact review/idempotence, stale or cross-chat names, live metadata changes and durable replay without re-reading. Its first response calls those reads and read_file, using 118 output tokens rather than the preceding run's 331. Input rises from 11,596 to 11,867 tokens. First-model time is 35.7 seconds, total journey 104.8 seconds and correction-to-audio 23.1 seconds, compared with 53.8, 136.5 and 35.5 seconds previously. Different machine load and output text prevent a controlled benchmark claim. The final actual file retains noon, room/code, sketchbook and $33, with two distinct writes and no repeated operations.

All six actual-model Mac app regressions pass. Visual review confirms readable source/result links and preserved drafts. The ordinary template reply still ends with an unnecessary file offer, and unavailable-source wording suggests reconnection without proving it necessary; both are retained as quality gaps. The voice evidence uses synthetic speech and deliberately holds the first completed write receipt to reproduce correction. `direct-documents-verification.json` indexes exact source/package checks, live receipts, comparison limits, unchanged Android APK/mobile assets, owner preservation and cleanup. Natural voice speed, physical acoustics, broader task reliability and the full JARVIS goal remain open.

File-reading tools now record source provenance on their file cards. The connector records resource/template kinds from its validated catalog; remote response text cannot supply that classification. ## Less model work before useful results — October 15, 2026

Explicit computer-file requests now prefetch a screenshot only when the owner's unquoted request refers to visible screen/window/control work. File-only requests receive a small, current power-state snapshot instead. An ownership change during that read discards the snapshot; an asleep computer is not started by the observation. All computer tools remain available, and actual screen requests retain their initial image. Only enabled/state/controller/network fields are supplied, with no unrelated error or receipt content. Selected static app documents also advertise their host-defined empty arguments, allowing direct exact-name reads and independent reads in one model response. Parameterized documents and tools still require schema lookup. Access, revisions, live metadata and exact review policy remain authoritative.

The actual Qwen 3.5 27B Quick voice/computer journey passes with all 53 tools, no image parts and six model requests. It reads both app sources plus the attachment, writes the brief, accepts a spoken correction in the same run, updates the same file, and verifies the exported bytes and phone-width preview. The observed task time is 136.5 seconds, versus 291.9 seconds for the prior accepted voice portion; correction-to-audio is 35.5 versus 56.7 seconds. Initial input falls from 13,003 to 11,596 tokens. These individual runs have different machine load and generated text, and the earlier run had an emulator running; they are not a controlled performance benchmark. The unchanged 120-second model deadline and prior cold-start failures remain relevant. This is still too slow for natural conversation.

The intermediate `voice-context-cost/first` run passed functionally but took 331.3 seconds. It skipped direct schema lookups, then unnecessarily called computer_start on a running desktop and reintroduced an image. The final power-state snapshot removed that detour in `voice-context-cost/power-state`. All model requests, exact operations, timings, exported content and manual screenshots remain in the receipts. The same current source passes six actual-model Mac app regressions: combined documents, parameterized note, hostile source instructions, changed metadata blocked before reading, unavailable content without guessing, and an explicitly chosen template kept in chat with the Main draft intact.

Four added regression cases cover visible-work selection, on-demand screen tools for file work, ownership/asleep handling and direct preview reads with stale-revision/review enforcement. The clean-source suite contains 1,008 tests. `voice-context-cost-verification.json` indexes source/package equality, live evidence, comparison limits, unchanged mobile/APK bytes, owner-state preservation and cleanup. There are no frontend or native changes and no fresh native-phone claim; the previous Android receipt remains tied to the unchanged APK. Physical acoustics, signed distribution, broader model reliability and the full JARVIS goal remain open.

## JARVIS voice, computer and device handoff — October 15, 2026

Speaking during an active voice request now holds work at its next safe boundary, then redirects the same run after recognition. Completed tool receipts survive; obsolete pending actions are skipped. Short rejected utterances resume work, failed recognition permits identical-audio retry, and explicit Interrupt/End still cancel. A spoken stop creates no model request and remains usable after a selected file disappears. Trusted voice provenance comes from the server, never a public command payload. A selected app no longer bypasses the actual computer-file delivery check when the owner explicitly requests private-computer output; quoted document names cannot activate it.

The integrated journey uses real local Whisper Base, Qwen 3.5 27B Quick, MCP reads, an isolated Linux computer and Mac speech with synthetic spoken input. It reads independently authored app documents and an uploaded note, creates a brief, corrects “book” to “sketchbook” during the same run, and verifies the actual file/export bytes and unpredictable room/code/time/cost. The first completed write receipt is deliberately held until the correction arrives; actual actions and model decisions are not mocked. The native Android APK previews the file and saves a follow-up draft; desktop sends that draft and answers with the corrected item in the same conversation.

This journey exposed a real draft handoff bug: an acknowledged desktop draft kept ignoring newer phone text. Idle editors now follow newer nonempty remote drafts while preserving unsaved/offline local edits and protecting an open draft from a remote Send clearing it. The strengthened two-browser draft regression and desktop/mobile session-recovery journeys pass. The latter also retain attachments, recover expired sessions and add no navigation messages. The Android controlled voice test (`android-voice-1792014815949.json`) passes its complete lifecycle plus a same-run correction while the model is working; all five actual device captures were inspected. Its APK SHA-256 is `e2ef61fea76146a9409972e91bfd00322f3189fde26c2a7940507851c38ad44c`.

Diagnostics remain under `voice-computer/`: `first` caught a filename observer mismatch after speech recognition; `mac-android` caught Electron's native download-event difference; `final` exposed the production draft bug; `verified-handoff` passed the complete functional journey but failed a cleanup-result type check. Earlier stopped display containers and their volumes were subsequently removed with exact run attribution. The corrected cleanup removes display containers before volumes and verifies absence. `release-acceptance` then hit the unchanged 120-second local-model timeout before any file work. The repeated cold-start failure remains a reliability gap, not an accepted performance result. The final `release-retry` journey passes every assertion and verified cleanup. It takes 291.9 seconds from the first utterance to the corrected spoken result, including the held receipt and correction, and 56.7 seconds from correction to audio. These are individual observations, not a controlled benchmark.

Four new voice tests cover same-run correction with retained completed work, short-utterance resume, failed-recognition retry/provenance and durable spoken stop. One new delivery test covers selected apps plus explicit computer output. These are part of the 1,004-test suite. The aggregate `voice-computer-verification.json` records final clean-source, native/package, visual, owner-preservation and cleanup results. Physical microphones, acoustic echo, physical phones, signed distribution and broad model reliability remain open; the JARVIS goal is active.

## JARVIS direction and actual Android acceptance — October 15, 2026

The owner changed the target to a JARVIS-style assistant. `JARVIS_GOAL.md` records the experience, measurable milestones and remaining integrated voice/context/computer scenario. The existing Relay implementation and original identity remain its foundation; no competitor or fictional-character assets/code were added.

The owner explicitly accepted the Android SDK license and authorized installation/testing. A private workspace SDK, existing JDK 21 and dedicated AOSP Android 16 ARM64 emulator built and ran the actual debug APK. Gradle also installed its default stable Build Tools 35 under that license. The original launcher/splash now uses a vector sprout mark. Invalid server input uses an accessible inline error instead of a tooltip over the form buttons.

Completed same-APK receipts: `android-app-1792004132935.json` verifies real system sharing from a separate fixture app, exact photo/document bytes, text, one manual Send, side-chat selection, page navigation, process relaunch, draft retention and embedded-WebView accessibility. `android-connection-1792004686760.json` verifies English/Simplified/Traditional Chinese native forms and offline recovery, retained invalid input, blocked ports, subresource-failure isolation, Retry, relaunch, Change server, Cancel, Use sample and restoration of server drafts with no model/chat events. `android-voice-1792005310303.json` verifies actual WebView audio processing, capture segmentation, playback, interruption, mute, identical-audio retry, stopped capture/playback, closed audio contexts and draft retention after relaunch. Voice uses an explicitly synthetic microphone and controlled HTTP providers.

Initial diagnostics remain saved: missing Playwright Android instrumentation; stale cached WebView pages after Activity recreation; escaped Android JSON; immediate native-selector timeouts because the current Playwright wire protocol omits inner timeouts; a stale system-chooser task; browser-only axe mode; reading the error-page URL or native dialog before it settled; and ignored regex case flags. The voice observer initially counted a source as active when Android closed its context before a final `ended` event. It now checks actual stop calls, source/context state and capture cleanup. These harness fixes do not substitute for the final successful native runs.

`jarvis-android-verification.json` indexes the final APK hash, SDK consent/packages, native receipts, source rebuild, source/native-input hashes, owner-state preservation and cleanup. APKs are development builds, not signed distribution releases. These checks do not certify physical microphones/camera/Bluetooth, live accounts, Android push, public TLS joining, arbitrary model quality, every native file-export path or the full product goal. See ANDROID_TESTING.md and VOICE_CALLS.md.

## Connected-app work with actual local models — October 15, 2026

Real 9B trials exposed two discovery problems: an invented combined document/tool name appeared as an empty app catalog, and a requested app note was searched for as a local file. Unknown exact lookups now explain recovery, while explicitly selected apps supply a bounded preview of saved entry names, titles and kinds. App data stays behind actual reads. Selected catalogs of at most 64 distinct names also provide exact name/connection choices in the existing model tool schemas. Host permissions, saved revisions, disabled entries, argument validation and live metadata guards remain authoritative. No UI step or standing permission is added. Reviewing the 27B answers also exposed a misleading connection error for changed metadata. A host-recorded pre-dispatch mismatch now survives transport sanitization as a fixed catalog-refresh explanation, with no remote error body or credentials.

Four added Node cases cover exact-name recovery, inaccessible/stale selection isolation, metadata-only bounded previews and fresh per-request tool definitions without global mutation, plus Unicode-bounded UTF-8 file previews, invalid/binary exclusion, exact original bytes and credential filtering before clipping. The live journey uses real local inference, production chat/provider/worker/persistence and an independent JSON/SSE app. Its six cases combine a text plan and binary CSV using unpredictable source values, read a parameterized daily note, ignore hostile source instructions, reject changed metadata before a content read, report an unavailable read without inventing data, and use a chosen template exactly once while preserving Main's unfinished draft through reload.

The initial 9B name lookup and file-routing failures are retained. A catalog-preview trial passed five cases but its template harness checked the old completed run before the new request was queued; the harness now waits for the new run. A repeat 9B trial still copied an incorrect tool identifier and stopped with an intention instead of the answer. Exact-name schema choices were added after that trial. The pre-choice 27B phone run and final current-source receipts are separately labelled. A later 9B run retrieved the CSV but guessed a formula without reading its bytes. Supported text blobs now include a 2,000-code-point preview in the same result, with truncation flagged and original files unchanged. Manual review then rejected an apparently passing 9B template result: it made an unrequested Word file and exposed an internal file ID. The original automated assertion only checked topic content. The final harness also requires an ordinary plan to remain in chat with no new file or internal ID; the direct-template context now states that default explicitly. These results do not establish universal small-model reliability, real vendor-account access or native phone acceptance.

Run `apps:content:test:live` (append `-- --mac` for the packaged client), with the independent acceptance cache/model environment described in APP_CONTENT.md. Evidence is under `../relay-evidence/app-content-live/`; the final `app-content-live-verification.json` indexes accepted runs, retained diagnostics, clean-source rebuild, package-byte equality and owner preservation. Mac connects its packaged renderer to an isolated current-source server; exact packaged runtime bytes are checked separately. Mobile web assets are synchronized, with no fresh iPhone/Android runtime claim for this increment. At that increment Android SDK consent was still pending; the later Android entry above records acceptance and scoped native runs. Physical devices, signed releases and other parity requirements remain open.

## Directly chosen app templates — October 15, 2026

Your apps → Templates is now a searchable separate page with editable fields, explicit completion choices and **Use in chat**. One choice retrieves the exact template and creates one labelled request, preserving all unrelated composer draft fields and without another template review. An original server module validates the exact connection/revision/entry/fields and current chat/assistant access, uses existing guarded JSON/SSE or isolated-stdio retrieval, and atomically records the resulting chat request with a durable choice receipt. Lost responses/restarts reuse it; changed arguments cannot reuse the same receipt. Cancelled or revoked work does not queue a late request. Completed retrieval can retain returned files even if a later cancellation prevents sending. Existing uncertain outcomes are not blindly replayed. Template source is labelled untrusted, generated labels cannot be learned as owner memory, and existing external-action reviews remain. Text-only models can consume retrieved text, and questions/app/computer continuations retain template context.

Six Node cases cover browsing/options without retrieval, all draft fields, duplicate receipt/restart behavior, cancellation/revocation/metadata changes, text-only model input, clarification, memory exclusion, group targeting and owner/same-origin/model-configuration routes. The original prompt-review and document-option tests remain regression checks. Packaged Mac and actual native iPhone Simulator journeys have separate receipts. The isolated stdio runtime now tests direct selection plus duplicate receipt reuse as well as the earlier prompt/resource protocols.

## Computer file delivery and action bookkeeping — October 14, 2026

An actual Qwen 3.5 9B run saved a checklist but ended without attaching it. Relay now applies a conservative completion check to explicit owner requests for a computer file in this chat. A current-request export, visible file card and matching content fingerprint are required. Claims are withheld; a private continuation asks the model to finish the remaining task and export the actual output. At most three corrections are allowed, each after the first requiring intervening computer-tool work. Consecutive unsupported claims fail. The original request and action history remain intact.

Explicit delivery requests allow 20 computer tool rounds rather than 16. Exhaustion before export becomes a failure with the existing retry control. Each explicit owner retry adds four rounds; process restart or reload does not reset the counter. Clarification answers retain their server-bound original request, while negation or a direct owner correction can cancel delivery. Screens, source text, app requests, routines and handoffs do not become new export authority. The check proves presence of at least one real result, not correctness of every edit or completeness of multi-file work.

Live trials also exposed missing action IDs and planning from computer filenames without seeing the open window. Relay now supplies one guarded initial screenshot/control observation for explicit file-delivery requests when available. It does not start apps or add history. Known text-only tool models retain the optional observation’s readable metadata without receiving its screenshot; required owner/tool images still fail clearly. Models may omit desktop operation IDs: the worker derives them from its durable tool-call identity and returns a separate exact retry key. Changed retries remain rejected; uncertain actions retain the existing recovery policy. Normal owner input IDs and API behavior are unchanged. A missing export path triggers bounded metadata lookup in the desktop and workspace Downloads folders; only exact-name regular files become hints, with no implicit export or filename substitution. Computer exports now label their source and return actual byte length, a bounded plain-text preview when available, and a reminder to answer remaining requested details from evidence.

Focused tests cover recognition boundaries, current-request evidence, edited/deleted and empty files, hidden false claims, bounded correction and retry, clarification, owner redirection, initial observations, text-only tool compatibility without dropping required images, stable generated IDs and explicit replay. The controlled UI journey covers desktop, 390-pixel and 320-pixel phone layouts: honest failure, one private correction, retained draft across reload and Try again, exact file bytes, separate preview/download, one original run/request, no horizontal overflow and accessibility. It uses a controlled HTTP model and an original fixture-file bridge; actual Docker and Ollama inference have separate receipts.

Run `computer:delivery:test:ui` for the controlled journey. The existing `computer:test:live -- --controls` exercises the real offline desktop with a chosen installed model/cache and dedicated engine; add `--mac` for the packaged renderer. Receipts, screenshots and failed trials are under `../relay-evidence/computer-delivery/`. Aggregate source, package, owner preservation and cleanup evidence is `computer-delivery-verification.json`. Earlier failures are retained: a 16-round run saved only at its last action; another launched an unnecessary browser job; a later model narrated plans without acting. A later 9B trial returned the exact checklist but omitted the requested destination in its final prose; the export preview and final-answer reminder address that observed gap without claiming universal semantic validation. Another trial looked for the download at the workspace root and substituted its source HTML; exact-name location hints now make the actual download discoverable after that error. The next 9B run repeatedly chose incorrect click coordinates and exhausted its budget; Relay reported failure and did not claim delivery. The small model therefore remains uncertified for this task. UI harness corrections switched from the edit-only textarea to the actual read-only preview and its text matcher. Failed attempts are not counted as end-to-end passes.

The final 27B browser run passed the complete randomized checklist task in 100 seconds: correct GUI state, exact app-saved attachment bytes, destination/reference in the reply, download, separate Computer page, takeover and retained draft after reload. This is one actual-model acceptance, not a small-model reliability or speed claim. The aggregate receipt records the matching final Mac and clean-source results separately.

This is independently implemented from observed Relay behavior. No competitor code or assets were imported. Native Android SDK consent, physical-device tests, broad model/task reliability and the remaining product parity ledger stay open. Mobile web assets are compared with the build; browser phone layouts are not native-phone evidence.

Neither failed journey is counted as an end-to-end pass. A first Mac run then used the valid startup screenshot; an older assertion incorrectly required a separate look tool. ## Missing computer storage and uninterrupted chat — October 14, 2026

Previously verified workspace storage is now remembered. Cancelling, changed storage and attached runtimes prevent replacement; failed startup rolls back the selected location. Existing operation journals preserve recovery across interruption. Chat provides **Recover computer**, and sending a message can continue without making a storage choice or creating a volume.
