## Computer documents from a plain chat request — October 14, 2026

Computer-file reads now decode DOCX, XLSX, PPTX and PDF content, paginate UTF-8 text, and identify unknown binaries without returning gibberish. A continuation requires the same content hash. Reading adds no library files or chat history; cancellation and owner takeover discard pending reads. Agent guidance distinguishes the computer filesystem from Relay's file library. The guest includes Debian's python-docx library and validates it with UNO during setup.

Live acceptance exposed two separate gaps: asking for an upload after checking only the library, and a Word script that replaced an entire paragraph and silently removed its bold label. The latter failed the test despite a successful model response and actual exports. Relay now has a bounded `computer_edit_docx` tool for unique text literals inside existing Word runs. It preserves unrelated package parts, writes only a new copy, rejects ambiguous or unsupported edits, and uses the existing operation receipt and takeover controls. Unit tests compare every ZIP part, exercise Unicode/XML escaping and UTF-16, and reject stale hashes, signed packages, unsupported whitespace and in-flight takeover.

The plain-chat harness checks an actual edited Word copy and independently parsed PDF, unchanged original bytes, retained table/bold/footer content, separate-page preview with draft retention, and honest failure on a corrupt source. The first browser pass took 282 seconds for the edit and 38 seconds for the damaged-file response; it used the earlier image and is retained separately. The final Mac run is recorded under `../relay-evidence/computer-office-live/27b-validated-mac/`. Earlier failures remain alongside it, including the failed bold-format assertion in `27b-final-mac` and `27b-precise-mac`, where the edit passed but LibreOffice interpreted corrupt input as text and produced an unsuitable PDF. The assistant guidance now requires a successful source read before conversion and explicitly forbids treating unreadable bytes as a valid substitute. This is model guidance, not a universal validator around arbitrary shell commands. A “done” response alone never passes acceptance.

The updated image also runs the nine actual office UI/runtime checks. Rebuild receipts, exact package/source comparisons, cleanup and owner-state preservation are collected in `../relay-evidence/computer-office-live-verification.json`. These are synthetic fixtures with dedicated local test weights, not owner accounts. This increment does not certify arbitrary Office fidelity, other models, native Android or physical devices. See [computer documents](COMPUTER_DOCUMENTS.md) for commands and supported edit boundaries.

## Office apps in the private computer — October 14, 2026

The existing Computer Files section now opens supported documents or creates a blank document, spreadsheet or presentation directly. The updated guest contains LibreOffice Writer/Calc/Impress/Draw, the UNO bridge, PDF text utilities and compatible fonts. Existing computers advertise no new controls until explicitly updated through Computer care. The fixed owner-only launch protocol validates regular workspace files, preserves operation receipts and freezes agent commands. Owner and agent office profiles are separate to avoid accidental forwarding into a frozen app.

Real production-browser and packaged Mac journeys each cover nine checks: actual DOCX edits including Unicode, XLSX/PPTX/PDF/text opening, blank-file creation and save, agent-side PDF conversion, the locked macro policy, rejected paths/app IDs, automatic file-list refresh with a deliberately delayed old-folder response, exact saved bytes after stop/start, and responsive/accessibility/history/draft behavior. The source tests add owner-only launch/replay and guest-capability lifecycle regressions. Browser screenshots at 320, 390 and 768 pixels and native iPhone screenshots were inspected.

Acceptance exposed two unnecessary default steps in LibreOffice: a presentation template chooser and saves into a hidden home folder. Relay's original configuration disables the startup chooser and sets both current and legacy default work paths to the visible workspace. The test saves a new ODT using only its filename and reads it from the root workspace. Macro execution is disabled through a finalized system setting; actual UNO access confirms it is true and rejects lowering it. This does not remove general-purpose programming tools from the isolated computer.

Native iPhone Simulator acceptance covers four checks against real LibreOffice: opening the DOCX, editing with the native soft keyboard, creating slides without a chooser, and retaining the draft/history across navigation and full relaunch. The first native attempt inspected selection before all queued typing finished; the final test waits for the production input queue's enabled control state before reading the guest selection. The failed bundle and final evidence are retained. Browser emulation is not treated as native evidence.

Cleanup also found four stopped command containers left by the earlier native-keyboard harness. That harness closed SQLite before stopping the computer; cleanup now runs first and removes both guest containers. Exact synthetic-editor hashes and deployment labels were checked before removing those older test volumes. The final source/package/owner-state aggregate is `computer-office-verification.json`; detailed receipts and screenshots are in `computer-office/`. These tests use synthetic documents, no owner accounts and no model calls. General Office compatibility, arbitrary app installation, physical devices and Android runtime remain outside this acceptance. Android web assets are synchronized. See [computer documents](COMPUTER_DOCUMENTS.md) for commands and update behavior.

## Direct phone keyboard for the private computer — October 14, 2026

The keyboard icon beside the existing text field opens live guest typing from a single explicit touch. It uses the existing ordered, bounded input queue; ordinary screen taps still leave the device keyboard closed. **Done** restores any unsent buffered text. On touch devices, live mode temporarily hides the app header and bottom dock, leaving Back to chat, the guest and Done accessible. This avoids crowding the desktop behind the native keyboard without adding a popup, setting or chat-history entry.

Soft-keyboard deletion and Return are handled as cancellable editing intentions, while physical keyboard handlers retain their existing behavior. A local sentinel enables deletion after each sent chunk and is stripped before guest input. Composition stays local until committed; closing the keyboard or losing control clears unfinished composition. Control loss closes the keyboard and retains existing queue cancellation and no-replay behavior. A regression caught React replacing Done with the buffered form's submit button before the browser's default click, which could send buffered text. Explicit click cancellation and the form's live-mode guard prevent that unintended submission.

The controlled production-UI journey covers 390-, 320- and 768-pixel layouts, direct focus, Unicode, exact deletion/Return, composition commit/cancel/reopen, loss of control, preserved buffered and chat drafts, unchanged history and accessibility. The real Docker desktop/phone journey checks actual guest text and window events, including direct Unicode plus deletion and newline, existing mouse typing, composition, dragging, invalid/cancelled gestures and durable replay boundaries. The packaged Mac client runs its existing direct-input regression against the same production backend.

Actual iPhone Simulator acceptance opens the soft keyboard through the UI, types into the Linux editor, deletes one character, inserts a newline, closes the keyboard, drags the guest window, fully relaunches and hands control back from chat. Exact guest bytes, original window identity, frozen owner runtime, unchanged chat and retained draft are checked. Native screenshots are inspected separately; browser emulation is not substituted for this evidence. The first native attempt queried the pressed keyboard control as a plain button; native accessibility exposes it differently, so the final selector uses its exact accessible label. The failed bundle is retained.

The source audit also caught the newly advertised keyboard test missing from the reviewed archive list. It is now included, and a regression checks that every script entrypoint advertised by package commands is shipped. Final source, package, platform and owner-state receipts are collected in `computer-keyboard-verification.json`. Tests use an original synthetic Linux editor and no live model/account. Android assets are synchronized only. Physical keyboards/devices, all mobile IMEs, Android runtime and broader computer/product gates remain open.

## App-template options without another chat exchange — October 14, 2026

Connected apps that advertise MCP completions now offer **Find options** beside each editable template field. The compact label row keeps the action reachable while the native keyboard is open. The native trace exposed a real resize issue: WebKit could shrink the chat while the focused field remained behind the composer. A scoped focus/viewport-resize handler now reveals the field and label inside the chat scroller, without pinning ordinary user scrolling or moving other pages. Choosing an option fills the existing field; **Use template** remains the final explicit choice. Typing stays local. An inline note names the app receiving an explicit lookup, with manual entry retained on empty results or errors. Eight options appear initially, with more available in place. Identical recent lookups use a bounded 30-second memory cache, and suggestions never enter chat history or persisted drafts. This is original Relay code implementing the [MCP completion protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/server/utilities/completion).

Every lookup revalidates the pending review, current app/access metadata and live completion capability. HTTP JSON/SSE and isolated stdio use the prompt reference, one field/value and other explicitly supplied string fields, including empty values. Responses are bounded and checked, duplicate values collapse, and known connection credentials are omitted. Per-app rate/concurrency limits and a global concurrency limit bound work. Cancellation, editing, navigation, approval, decline and access changes discard outstanding results. No lookup retrieves or approves a template. Five added backend cases exercise these boundaries; owner authentication and same-origin protection are also checked.

The browser journeys cover desktop and phone layout, focused-field visibility after keyboard-sized viewport changes, cancellation, late replies, lookup errors, empty results, literal text, cached results, expanded choices, retained draft values and a single exact final template retrieval. Manual-entry journeys cover apps without completions. The packaged Mac client runs the same lookup flow. The isolated Docker journey executes an original Python stdio app, checks exact completion parameters and retrieval, and verifies cancellation stops its guest process. Fixtures use original synthetic content and deterministic HTTP model responses, not live vendor accounts.

Native acceptance uses the actual iPhone Simulator app over protected HTTPS, with a keyboard lookup, selection, navigation and full relaunch before exact template retrieval. Diagnostic attempts are retained separately: accessibility marked clipped content hittable, large gestures overshot the small viewport, overly strict visibility bounds excluded visible controls, and Simulator hardware Select All sometimes failed. The final test uses keyboard End and backspace for the short synthetic value, checks actual viewport bounds and exact field values, asserts no lookup before the explicit tap, and verifies one lookup plus one retrieval. The compact field action replaces the earlier below-field layout; experimental focus interception was removed. Optional fixture-only input tracing records geometry and event targets for diagnosis.

Final clean-source, package, mobile assets, native/runtime evidence and owner-state receipts are collected in `prompt-options-verification.json`. Android assets are synchronized only. Physical devices, Android runtime, live-account/model quality, resource-template completions, resource browsing and the broader product acceptance scope remain open.

These are production integration tests using synthetic content and deterministic model responses; they do not certify vendor accounts, physical devices or live model quality. ## Simpler result pages — October 14, 2026

Saved results now keep **Download / Save file** and **Ask about this** together, with a 44-pixel edit button beside the title. **Back to chat** and **All files** share one navigation row. Version, sheet and slide selectors appear only when there is a choice; editing retains the controls to add sheets and slides. Unsaved previews say **Draft** and hide actions that would download or discuss the previous saved bytes. Discard restores those actions and the original content without adding chat messages.

The new-result navigation test exposed a real initialization race: the result route could arrive before the workspace update, leaving an editor initialized with an empty starter. The stateful editor now mounts only after the requested saved result/version is available. The delayed-data regression, draft recovery, stale-version protection, revision history and original download checks pass with the final build.

The result acceptance suite passes **21 checks in the browser run** and **12 in the Mac run**: 18 desktop/phone-browser scenarios, nine packaged-Mac scenarios, and three additional browser sandbox/platform guards in each run. Coverage includes original four-format exports, editing, multiple sheets/slides, native download bytes, accessibility and 320-pixel phone layout. Actual iPhone Simulator acceptance edits a document, previews the unsaved draft, discards it, then saves DOCX, XLSX, PPTX and HTML through the system Files picker. Every exported byte matches the original. Picker cancellation, interactive native preview, stop/restart, Back, chat-draft preservation and cleanup after force-quit/relaunch also pass. No live model is called in these integration checks.

Two native test attempts are retained as failures. The first attempted Preview behind the fixed toolbar; the reveal helper now accounts for that toolbar. The second assumed an editor-mode toggle had succeeded before discarding and waited for a Preview button after returning to reading mode. The final test uses the simpler supported flow: edit, preview the draft, discard directly from that preview, then export the unchanged file. It retains the unsaved-file guard and exact-byte checks. Earlier browser/build attempts retain the initialization failure and the TypeScript annotation correction; none are counted as passing acceptance.

Final receipts are collected in `results-simple-verification.json`, with individual results under `results-simple/`, `results-simple-mac/` and `results-simple-ios/`. The clean-source rebuild runs all 907 backend tests; the aggregate verifies packaged runtime and mobile web assets against the tested build and records owner-state preservation. ## Generated-file content and script checks — October 14, 2026

Saved-result feedback now pairs the bound owner request with the content preview. Spreadsheet checks distinguish editable formulas and their calculated values from formula-looking text; they never silently rewrite a cell. File instructions clarify exact supplied values, new results versus revisions, structured document headings and the interactive preview’s runtime limits. This adds no user question, popup or setup step.

Interactive exports now syntax-check inline classic JavaScript and common inline event handlers before committing a file or revision. The code is compiled without execution. Malformed source returns a repair error through the normal tool loop; a corrected save produces one result card. Old versions and bytes stay unchanged on failure. An exact malformed page retained from a real 4B-model run is rejected with zero files created. Comments, text areas, data scripts, modules, resource bounds and a non-executed throwing/infinite-loop source are covered. The actual local-model trials deliberately retain failures. Initial Qwen 3.5 4B Quick-mode runs changed a supplied time, stored a formula as text or confused a new spreadsheet with a document revision. Model-default reasoning passed Word, formula-backed Excel and PowerPoint with notes in several runs, but generated interactive code still used unavailable storage or had malformed JavaScript. Prompt improvements and static checks do not establish that this small model is reliable for every task. No owner model, reply setting, weight download or conversation was changed.

The live harness also supports `--mac`: the packaged client connects to an isolated production backend, downloads actual Office bytes, and shares the conversation with a phone browser. It verifies editable formula totals, slide notes, real interactive control behavior, phone fit and an exact revision that preserves the original source and bytes. Button matching accepts a descriptive accessible name such as **Reset to zero**, while still requiring the counter to change from two to zero; an earlier overly strict locator failure is retained. This does not certify physical/native mobile devices or the Mac bundled backend’s local-model setup.

`artifact-content-verification.json` aggregates the exact live model/mode and scenarios, retained attempts, clean-source tests and rebuild, package byte matches, screenshots and owner-preview preservation. The 4B semantic/runtime failures, unchecked module/CSS/DOM behavior, Android app runtime, physical devices and signed distribution remain open gates. The product goal remains active.

These attempts are not counted as passing acceptance.

An actual Qwen3.5:27b Quick baseline passed only one of five proactive-help cases: malformed JSON, an invalid topic ID and prose in place of an empty list caused the other checks to fail honestly. ## Saved-file completion and live artifact workflow — October 14, 2026

A live Qwen3.5:27b Quick baseline created Word, Excel and PowerPoint results, then claimed an interactive HTML file was ready without calling any tool or saving a file. The final backend suite passes **862/862**. Forty focused artifact-completion, handoff and steering checks pass, including durable recovery, chat-only models, missing bytes, unrelated requests, owner follow-ups, same-assistant continuations and specialist receipts. Controlled desktop and 390-pixel phone-browser journeys verify two unsupported claims followed by an inline failure, draft-preserving reload and explicit retry, one original request/run/result, functional JavaScript on a separate result page, no dialog, no added navigation messages and no browser errors. The packaged arm64 Mac also passes this journey against the isolated test backend; its receipt is `artifact-completion/mac/artifact-completion-acceptance.json`. This is native-shell UI evidence, not a new bundled-backend or real-model run. Browser receipts are under `artifact-completion/`. The scripted failure deliberately tests integration behavior and is separate from actual model acceptance.

The actual **Qwen3.5:27b Quick workflow passes all five scenarios** using ordinary chat requests and the production worker/tools: Word document (106.6 seconds), Excel budget with editable SUM formula and total 54 (29.1 seconds), exactly three PowerPoint slides with speaker notes (82.2 seconds), an offline phone-sized counter with working Add one/Reset controls (153.7 seconds), and a Word revision changing only 10:30 AM to 11:15 AM (155.7 seconds). Independent export readers verify Office content, the entire revised source is compared, and the original export hash remains unchanged. This successful live run saved its results normally; automatic correction of an actual bad claim is established by the controlled tests, not by claiming this run invoked it. Earlier 9B failures remain evidence of model-quality limits.

Evidence: `artifacts-live/27b-quick-grounded/artifacts-live-acceptance.json` and its synthetic transport trace. Both final Word versions were rendered to one page each and all three PowerPoint slides were independently rendered and visually inspected: requested facts are readable without clipping or overlap. The renderer substitutes an available serif font for unavailable Office fonts; this is not a claim of pixel-identical Office rendering. Phone interaction is browser evidence; no physical-device, native-phone, Android or hosted-model acceptance is inferred. Final release and owner-preview receipts are collected in `artifact-completion-verification.json`.

## Grounded memory removal and direct recovery — October 14, 2026

Local Ollama memory selection in supported Quick mode now returns exact saved text under a JSON schema, then passes the existing host ownership, stale-note, cancellation and atomic receipt checks. Model default and other providers retain their existing selection protocol. Bare unresolved references skip inference; a trailing presentation-only instruction is excluded from selection. Failed or ambiguous selections expose a My memories link in the reply, preserving the draft and opening no automatic dialog.

The final backend suite passes **846/846**. Controlled recovery journeys pass on desktop, phone and the packaged Mac shell against an isolated HTTP backend. They deliberately inject an invalid selector reply, verify no deletion or false success, manually remove the intended note using the message link, retain the unrelated note, and exercise side-chat recall, reload, ambiguity, exact-all and empty-state handling. An initial phone run used the previous web build; a later desktop run exposed an ambiguous test locator shared with the header shortcut. The corrected test targets the message link and both layouts pass.

Actual Qwen 3.5 27B weights downloaded through Relay into a dedicated test cache. **Quick mode passes 17/17 semantic-selection cases and the full five-turn phone-browser chat journey.** Checks cover multiple subjects, identity versus preferences, English/Chinese exclusions, untrusted instructions within a note, no match, conditional requests, quoted text, vague references and exact-all. Warm inference selections took about 2–6 seconds; initial loading took about 22 seconds. A separately owned assistant’s notes remain unchanged. These cases do not certify arbitrary model judgment.

Small-model limitations remain: the rejected structured-output prototype with Qwen 3.5 9B in Model default passed **11/17**, with six 60-second timeouts and no deletions on those failures. Its Quick-mode full chat journey missed an explicit hotel target even after removing the presentation suffix. Earlier 15/15 prototype results forced Quick internally despite a default-mode label; they are not normal-reasoning evidence. The 27B normal-reasoning prototype was stopped after 7 cases: 2 passed and 5 reached the 60-second limit. Remaining cases were not run. The constrained path is therefore restricted to supported Quick mode; Model default preserves the original selector, and no model or reply setting is silently changed. The final native and release checks are recorded below.

The browser regression run passed 91 checks with two existing skips. One mobile page-load check overlapped a production rebuild; it passes when rerun with stable assets, giving 92 effective passing checks. The native iPhone run initially timed out during fresh Simulator migration before the app launched; the harness now permits five minutes for initial boot, with a retry recorded separately.

The first final default-mode chat retry removed the correct note and recalled the retained preference, but timed out on a vague future reference and generated an incorrect capability claim. Bare references with narrow future-only qualifiers now bypass selection and receive a host-generated clarification, including bare Chinese references. Descriptive subjects and compound tasks retain normal interpretation; the focused 31-test suite passes after this change.

Final live chat acceptance passes all five turns in **9B Model default** with the preserved selector and in **27B Quick** through the packaged Mac shell connected to an isolated backend. Desktop and phone recovery also pass again after the clarification fix. These are separate from the failed normal-reasoning structured-output prototypes above. Evidence: `9b-default-release-chat`, `27b-quick-final-mac`, and `recovery-release`.

The final iPhone Simulator run passes **nine native lifecycle checkpoints** with the current client and real HTTP/SQLite: learning with source evidence, source inspection and protected editing, Keep mine, Use update, learning-off across relaunch, manual notes, explicit saving while off, and direct forgetting from chat. The controlled model does not establish live-model reasoning quality. The fresh-boot retry succeeded after allowing five minutes for Simulator migration. Result bundle: `memory-native-runs/memory-lifecycle-native-1791922935291.xcresult`; receipt: `memory-lifecycle-native.json`. Physical devices, signing and Android runtime acceptance remain outside this check.

Evidence is under `relay-evidence/memory-grounding/`: `27b-quick`, `27b-chat-quick`, `9b-preserved-default`, `fallback-ui-final`, and `fallback-mac`. The owner workspace was not used for synthetic prompts or model downloads.

## Reasoning-model memory selection — October 14, 2026

The previous forget-selection deadline was too short for the installed Qwen3.5:9b model in its normal reasoning mode. Matched probes returned correct selections after roughly 22–43 seconds; the old 20-second boundary discarded such decisions. Explicit owner forget requests now have a bounded 60-second, 2,400-output-token allowance. Source authorization, assistant scope, atomic identity checks, cancellation, stale-control rejection and receipt replay are unchanged. Ordinary chats do not incur this selection pass. A no-selection result now tells the replying model that direct chat removal is available and to clarify the target without falsely claiming a capability limitation or requiring a settings visit.

An actual Qwen3.5:9b Model default connection with action tools disabled passes the five-turn phone journey twice after the allowance change. The final run also passes the stronger check against claiming that deletion is unavailable in chat: requested hotel note removed, unrelated music note retained and recalled in a fresh side chat, an ambiguous future preference left unchanged with clarification, exact-all removal, honest empty state, one memory link and retained drafts. The first pass's ambiguous reply still incorrectly described direct removal as unavailable; that exposed the context wording issue fixed before the final run. The final wording correctly offers clarification. Evidence: `memory-forgetting/9b-reasoning-budget/` and `memory-forgetting/9b-reasoning-final/`.

The new independent `memory:selection:test:live` suite records actual deleted identities against declared targets, exact remaining-note content, another assistant’s private note, learning-off state and the real no-tools transport. Qwen3.5:9b Model default passes **7 of 10** cases: music synonym/deletion wording, two subjects, an explicit exclusion, Chinese, no matching subject, an ambiguous target and exact-all bypass. All-preferences, future-condition and embedded-note-instruction cases time out and fail semantic acceptance; their notes remain unchanged. No unintended note is removed in this suite, but three failed cases are not counted as success merely because their failure was safe. Evidence: `memory-selection/9b-default/`. Other topics, languages and models remain unverified by this suite.

Exploratory probes compare the existing prompt, more explicit owner-field wording and a shorter prompt. A second controlled comparison holds the prompt constant: published nonthinking sampling passes 8/10 repeated cases, a lower-temperature variant passes 6/10, and Model default passes 9/10 with one timeout. These are small diagnostic samples, not a reliable ranking or proof that sampling fixes model judgment. [Qwen's model card](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B) documents different thinking/nonthinking sampling choices; production sampling and saved reply-mode defaults remain unchanged. Raw synthetic evidence stays in `memory-forgetting/selection-probe.json` and `memory-forgetting/sampling-probe.json`.

The focused 35 memory tests and 62 combined memory/model-capability/worker tests pass. Desktop/phone controlled HTTP journeys cover the same behavior. Clean source and current packaged-runtime verification are recorded in `memory-selection-verification.json`. This improves real inference acceptance without certifying arbitrary model judgments. Android SDK consent/setup remains pending and was rechecked; the proposed local SDK and license files do not exist. Physical devices, signing and remaining product parity are still open. The owner’s preview, provider choices and computer control are preserved.

## Direct forgetting with chat-only models — October 13, 2026

Specific forget requests now use a bounded note-selection pass before the assistant reply, including connections without action tools. Relay rechecks the active owner message, assistant scope, memory control version and frozen note contents before atomic deletion. The same transaction records an ID-only receipt and invalidates older pending learning. A retry or restart cannot apply the deletion to a replacement note. Exact all-memory commands bypass selection inference; qualified requests and all-preference requests do not bypass it. No new popup, page or confirmation step is introduced.

Simple English/Chinese forget commands use the recorded operation result for a short host-authored reply. They need no second model call and cannot turn an invalid selection into a false success claim. Compound requests continue through the normal assistant with the recorded outcome; their prose remains model-dependent. Failed or ambiguous selections leave notes untouched. The existing Memory forgotten link opens the current personal notes. Original chats, files and backups remain retained.

Thirty-five focused memory tests and 51 combined memory/assistant/group tests pass. The new coverage includes source authorization, learning-on/off, cross-assistant scope, exact-all qualification, malformed/duplicate/out-of-range selections, concurrent edits, cancellation, atomic SQLite reopen, replacement-note replay, stale extraction, real no-tools transport, truthful failure replies and compound-task continuation. Production desktop and 390-pixel phone browser journeys pass against an independent controlled HTTP model, including fresh-chat recall, unrelated-note preservation, ambiguous targets, empty state and draft reload. The expanded native iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Simulator journey passes nine storage checkpoints and four full relaunches with a controlled model and real agent/database. The packaged arm64 Mac app’s own backend also passes direct forgetting, unrelated-note preservation and full quit/relaunch with its draft; all 208 runtime files match the working source. The full clean backend suite passes 840 tests. Physical devices and Android remain unverified.

The actual Qwen3.5:9b Quick trial initially returned prose instead of a selection and then falsely claimed deletion while SQLite retained the note. Its trace exposed two integration issues: a generic no-tools instruction conflicted with the selection task, and a second model reply could invent success. The final transport has a selection-specific JSON prompt and simple replies come from the host's recorded operation. A final Quick trial returned an empty selection for a clear hotel request; Relay honestly asked which note and kept both notes. A Model default trial exceeded the 20-second selection deadline; Relay honestly offered My memories and kept both notes. **Neither final live trial passes semantic acceptance.** Production sampling and model defaults are unchanged. This increment verifies storage, transport, UI and honest failure handling, not reliable note selection by this model.

Evidence: `memory-forgetting/memory-forgetting-acceptance.json`, `memory-forgetting/9b-live/` (initial failure), `memory-forgetting/9b-final/` (Quick no-match), `memory-forgetting/9b-reasoning/` (timeout), and `memory-lifecycle-native.json`. The current packaged Mac, synchronized mobile assets and clean source archive are tracked in `memory-forgetting-verification.json` and its linked receipts. All acceptance data is synthetic and isolated from the owner's workspace. Broader model judgment, Android runtime/license consent, physical devices and signing remain open.

## Explicit memory with chat-only models — October 13, 2026

A live 9B Quick baseline saved and corrected a dinner preference but later invented organic/grass-fed preferences despite the correct current note being present in the outgoing system message. Its initial “vegetarian” dinner also contained shrimp. The run fails the existing recall gate; the food contradiction was found by inspecting the actual answer. Replaying the same captured recall prompt then returned the right fact, as did four experimental sampling/context variants. This does not isolate a deterministic prompt or storage defect and does not establish a sampling fix. Production sampling, tool availability and reasoning defaults remain unchanged. Synthetic traces remain under `memory-reliability/9b-baseline/` and `memory-reliability/context-probe.json`.

The investigation found two concrete integration inconsistencies: a chat-only model's normal instructions denied saving memory even though the host already runs a JSON-based memory review, and that review skipped direct remember requests when learning was off. The host now permits only an explicit current-owner save request through the existing source gate in that state. New or updated notes receive manual protection; existing owner wording still produces a proposal. Tool arguments cannot grant this exception. Explicit batches commit their notes and control-version change atomically, invalidating older pending extraction. Reaffirming an already learned fact promotes it to a protected manual note without duplication. Successful unchanged review also suppresses a redundant save tool. The chat-only prompt now describes the host's actual behavior and uses only saved facts for future-recall claims, while keeping direct editing/deletion in My memories.

Twenty-four focused memory tests and 51 combined memory/model-capability/worker tests pass. New cases cover the actual no-tools model adapter, one inspectable memory card, direct saves with learning off, ordinary-statement suppression, exact evidence, generated/scheduled/cancelled/archived sources, in-flight owner edits, protected manual origin, repeated requests, durable replay and no redundant save tool. Desktop and 390-pixel production UI tests pass with an independent controlled HTTP model; the existing automatic-learning, correction and owner-control UI journeys also pass.

An actual installed Qwen3.5:9b Quick connection with action tools disabled passes the three-turn explicit-save scenario through the phone composer: saved hotel preference with exact provenance, recall in a fresh side chat, and an unrelated ordinary preference left unsaved while learning stays off. Saved note and draft survive reload. The recall includes an unnecessary explanation of the memory setting; broad conversational quality is still open. Evidence: `memory-reliability/9b-explicit/` and `memory-reliability/9b-explicit-final/`. No owner model, credentials or history are used by these tests.

The expanded iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Simulator memory journey passes in 248.299 seconds with eight storage checkpoints and three full relaunches. It verifies an explicit source-linked manual save while learning stays off, retained drafts, correction reviews and forgetting. Its source-inspection flow preserves an older reading position; the final harness uses the existing Latest messages button to reach a newer card. The packaged Mac’s own backend passes six checks, including an explicit update with manual protection, another full quit/relaunch and independent Main/side-chat drafts. The initial Mac extension incorrectly expected relaunch to open the side chat; the final journey verifies Main’s draft before reopening the saved side chat. It also waits for the memory dialog to close before continuing. These tests use controlled model decisions with real storage and native UI, not general model judgment. Receipts: `memory-lifecycle-native.json` and `memory-local-mac.json`; final iPhone screenshot: `memory-native-explicit.png`. Source-release and package receipts separately verify the delivered code. ## Direct filename reads and conversation acceptance — October 13, 2026

`read_file` now opens an exact unique workspace filename or an exact ID. This removes an unnecessary lookup/copy step when the owner already names a file. The live harness checks all five cases even after a quality failure and records complete synthetic results. Its length checks are intentionally scoped rather than a universal language judge. ## Large model downloads and stricter memory acceptance — October 13, 2026

Long local-model downloads no longer fail after accumulating 32 MiB of otherwise valid progress events. Relay discards each parsed event and retains the existing per-line/catalog limits, two-hour deadline, two-minute inactivity timeout, explicit cancellation and installed-model verification. No new controls or setup steps are added. Nine focused download tests pass, including a generated stream exceeding the former cap and continuous valid progress stopped by the overall deadline. Desktop and 390-pixel phone flows pass cancellation/retry, reload, explicit activation, retained drafts, light/dark accessibility and absence of navigation messages or overflow.

An actual official `qwen3.5:9b` transfer stopped near 75% of its 7,639,229,431-byte model file. The retry completed and emitted 10,844,718 bytes across 116,612 progress events for the remaining portion, with no provider-reported error. This supports the cumulative limit as the cause of the first stop; the initial attempt did not retain its exact abort reason. The successful retry predates this fix and is evidence of model installation, not a full post-fix transfer. The generated long-stream test directly covers the removed limit. See `memory-model-comparison/download.json` and `large-model-download-verification.json`.

The live memory harness now rejects unsolicited files, tasks and question cards as well as checking saved notes and recall. It records these artifacts and safely handles an interrupted response capture. A model-default reasoning override for the focused review and lower test-only sampling did not establish reliable behavior, so production reasoning and sampling remain unchanged. A 4B trial saved the correct note but created an unwanted preference file and later consulted its stale contents. A 9B Quick retry saved the note but created an unrequested recipe file for a simple dinner suggestion. Both fail the stronger quality gate. An earlier 9B attempt stopped on a test false negative for valid “do not contain mushrooms” wording; the matcher now handles that phrase. These results supersede any interpretation of an older seven-turn storage pass as conversational quality acceptance.

All inference uses synthetic disposable workspaces and an isolated runtime/cache; the owner's active model and history are unchanged. This server-only increment leaves web/mobile assets unchanged. Broader memory quality, other live providers, physical devices, Android runtime/license consent and signed distribution remain open.

## One-tap failed reply recovery — October 13, 2026

Failed owner replies now retain bounded safe checkpoints and expose one inline Try again action. The same run resumes after confirmed tools, preserving the original request and attachments, unsent draft, saved result cards and external review boundaries. Retry uses the current model selection with private provider-state scoping; no source user message is duplicated. Transient service outages now ask the user to try again instead of incorrectly directing them to change credentials.

The focused backend suite covers SQLite restart, provider changes, attachment/draft retention, duplicate retry, uncertain actions, expiration/retention limits, owner takeover and cancellation, original-assistant membership, latest corrections, email review without send, authentication and same-origin enforcement. Desktop, 390px and 320px browser journeys use controlled HTTP model interruptions with real note creation, recovery, keyboard access, reload and separate-page navigation. All 798 backend checks pass. Packaged Mac and native iPhone Simulator retry/relaunch flows pass. A natural-language Qwen3.5:4b Quick replies trial created exact random file content, survived one deliberately injected HTTP 503, and resumed through the phone browser without another file or user message (21.7 seconds overall). Its first harness run completed the same recovery, but the assertion incorrectly matched raw Markdown against rendered text; the visible bubble assertion was corrected, and that failed harness record is retained. Native/package/source checks and any limitations are recorded in `chat-retry-verification.json`. This is recovery verification, not a claim that any model will always avoid proposing duplicate work. Physical devices, Android runtime/license consent, signed distribution, broader live-provider quality and remaining parity stay open.

## Live result creation and clearer tool inputs — October 13, 2026

## Artifact continuation and exact edits — October 13, 2026

Raw synthetic inference diagnostics exposed an adapter gap: Ollama returned `reasoning`, while Relay only retained `reasoning_content`. Both now survive tool rounds under their original keys, remain hidden from chat and are bounded for JSON and streaming responses. Existing connection-scoping strips this private state on provider/revision changes. After this fix, a focused Model default trial returned visible answers but changed unrelated line breaks during revision.

The artifact tool now accepts exact find/replace edits against the saved source and version. It rejects missing/ambiguous matches, conflicting formats, malformed content, wrong identity and stale revisions before any save. Every successful revision retains its original source/export. Main and side follow-ups now receive their own recent saved-result references; these are separate context messages rather than assistant prose, and remain within the existing chat cutoff. No new owner step or popup is added.

The live harness now records raw synthetic transport evidence, checks whole-source preservation and rejects leaked internal result IDs. Its previous read-before-revise assertion accepted only read_artifact; read_file also returns the generated source, so either correctly targeted read now qualifies. A focused Quick replies trial passed. A full Quick trial falsely claimed an Excel file was ready. A Model default development run produced all four valid formats, a working counter and an exact revision using edits, but review found echoed context metadata in one reply. A repeat made one slide instead of three. Context separation was corrected afterward; this history remains part of the evidence rather than a general reliability claim.

Final backend/browser/Mac checks, the final live trial, source archive/package identity and owner-state preservation are recorded in `artifacts-continuation-verification.json`. This is a shared-server increment; web/native client code is unchanged. Physical devices, Android runtime/license consent, broader live model/accounts, signed distribution and the broader product goal remain open.

Ordinary chat requests now have a dedicated real-model acceptance harness: create a Word document with a random reference, an Excel budget with independently checked formulas, a three-slide PowerPoint with notes, a working offline counter, then revise the original document while preserving its prior bytes. Requests contain no tool names or schemas. Desktop and phone browsers open actual cards, download and inspect the generated files, preserve drafts and avoid navigation messages. Failure records are retained; a completed model turn is not treated as a passed result.

The trials exposed an actual capability mismatch: an unavailable terminal was still offered and could create a dead-end command review for a simple Word request. It is now filtered and checked again on execution. The built-in artifact tool accepts structured sheets/slides and a simpler single-sheet CSV format, rejects ambiguous representations, gives useful retry errors, and returns bounded computed content checks. Stored sources, HTTP editor inputs and immutable revision rules are unchanged. Workspace-file guidance now distinguishes local results from connected Google files. No extra user setup, confirmation or chat control was added.

All **782 backend tests pass**, including structured Office exports and revisions, CSV formulas/quoting/literal identifiers, competing/invalid input rejection, bounded content feedback and terminal availability. Development failures are summarized in `artifacts-live-development-trials.json`. Qwen3.5:4b is not certified for this workflow: Quick replies trials showed malformed arguments, wrong ranges/alignment and mistaken capability claims; Model default created correct Office results in the latest trial but claimed an interactive page existed without saving one. Those are real model-quality failures, not successful exports. The first Qwen3.5:9b / Model default trial produced all four valid formats and a working counter, but created disconnected copies during revision. The model save path now rejects same-kind, same-title new copies inside the storage transaction and returns the existing revision target. A later full trial omitted requested notes on two slides; the complete five-request model-quality gate therefore remains open. Focused revision trials exposed a wrong-next-version call and a later empty completion. File reads now return explicit revision fields, and version errors distinguish an outdated source from an incorrectly supplied next version. Empty model completion without a saved result now fails visibly instead of silently completing; result-only replies remain valid. Controlled browser recovery and final package/source identity are recorded in `artifacts-live-verification.json`. None of these checks certifies reliable end-to-end generation by either tested local model. Physical devices, Android, broader live-provider quality, signing and remaining parity stay open.

## Native result saving and interrupted-export cleanup — October 13, 2026

The actual iPhone Simulator app now has an end-to-end result export acceptance: protected HTTPS workspace → original chat result card → separate result page → system Files picker → saved DOCX/XLSX/PPTX/HTML. This closes generated Office/HTML export acceptance on iPhone Simulator; physical devices, Android runtime, live-model artifact quality and signed distribution remain open.

Each normal request produces exactly one model call, with no tool payload, memory write or new UI step. Both model requests use the advertised quick control; elapsed time is **13.859 seconds**. This verifies an actual action rather than accepting a claim that a file was written.

The complete backend suite passes **771 tests**. Updated desktop/phone model-capabilities journeys check new defaults, changing Reply style, persisted model defaults, supported wire fields, real local memory actions through controlled model responses, draft preservation, accessibility and fit. Final Mac/iPhone Simulator acceptance, package/source identity, clean-source rebuild and owner-state preservation are recorded in `quick-replies-verification.json`. ## Separate hosted computer and draft recovery — October 13, 2026

Relay can attach an administrator-managed dedicated Linux VM through pinned **native dockerd mutual TLS**. A bounded private connection file supplies an immutable image ID and stable workspace ID; incompatible environment settings and already-used computer history are rejected. The credential grants the whole dedicated engine, so the VM must remain exclusive to this workspace. See REMOTE_COMPUTER.md.

**749/749 Node tests pass**, including four remote-configuration cases. `remote-computer-runtime-acceptance.json` records nine passing checks on two actual ARM64 Linux VMs: client/server certificate rejection, real desktop screenshots, once-only commands and agent file export, detached-process freezing with usable owner controls, approved/revoked public HTTPS, separate mount-free private Chromium, actual volume capture/restoration, application replacement retaining control/history/draft, and actual MCP stdio discovery plus a reviewed Unicode file write. Tests start/take control, execute a real command, stop/start the same remote computer with exact file bytes, hand it back, receive an agent-produced file and retain the authenticated session and draft across app-server replacement. Page navigation/control creates no chat messages.

Only the disposable Simulator trusts the test HTTPS CA. Initial harness attempts needed WebKit toggle selectors and scrolling outside the interactive guest screen; they are not counted as successful runs.

Paired-device acceptance exposed an idle editor re-saving stale text over another device's newer draft. The draft hook now writes only genuine pending local edits, serializes saves and keeps the most recent keystroke backup until acknowledgement. Existing unfinished text stays in its open editor when another device sends. A two-device server regression, four desktop/phone reload/last-keystroke tests and both session-recovery journeys pass, including offline edits, expired-session recovery, attachments and independent device synchronization. The final remote phone journey retains its newer draft while the original desktop editor remains open.

The production web build and Linux image build pass. `remote-computer-image-identity.json` verifies all **180 server/shared/web runtime files** against the source, Node 24.21.0 ARM64, the pinned Docker 29.8.2 client, retained local-listening engine and exclusion of private build inputs. Final Mac/iOS source/package identity, own-backend Mac smoke, owner-state preservation and disposal of both test VMs/certificates are recorded by the corresponding `remote-computer-*` receipts and aggregate verification. The ordinary local computer configuration remains supported.

## Linux HTTPS hosting — October 13, 2026

`docs/HOSTING.md` documents the new Compose deployment and complete data-plus-secrets backup workflow. The original server Dockerfile could not build through the root private-computer-only `.dockerignore`; a separate Dockerfile allowlist now admits only server build inputs. Node/Caddy base images are pinned to official manifest digests. The app runs as UID 1000 with a read-only image, dropped capabilities, named data/secret volumes and no direct HTTP port, Docker socket or host bind mounts. Caddy provides the HTTPS entry point. First-start keys are generated atomically and existing keys are never silently replaced.

**741/741 Node tests** pass, including eight new secret/setup cases: environment compatibility, ambiguous settings, newline/projected mounts, bounded malformed-file rejection, nonblocking FIFO failure, restrictive key permissions, concurrent initialization, damaged-key preservation and symlink rejection. The production web build, Capacitor sync and production iPhone Simulator build pass. This adds hosting configuration rather than changing the conversation UI.

`hosting-acceptance.json` records seven passing end-to-end checks against an actual ARM64 Linux VM running Node **24.21.0**. Tests verify trusted TLS, secure session cookies, unauthenticated/origin rejection, rootless/read-only containers, worker chat transport, encrypted model configuration after container replacement, a one-shot routine completing exactly once after downtime with no browser open, actual Linux Chromium public-page reading/private-address rejection, and desktop/phone chat synchronization. Navigation creates no chat messages; both layouts have no overflow and zero tested WCAG A/AA violations. Model responses are from a controlled HTTP provider; these checks do not establish model reasoning quality.

`hosting-native-ios-acceptance.json` records one successful native iPhone 17 Pro/iOS 26.5 Simulator test, **49.224 seconds**, zero failures. The production WebKit app trusts the Linux Caddy root only in its disposable Simulator keychain. Native keyboard entry sends one message; the signed-in session and unsent draft survive a full app relaunch, actual Linux app container replacement and another app relaunch. Its test Simulator was deleted afterward.

`hosting-restore-acceptance.json` verifies the documented archive commands: stop the app, archive both volumes privately, reject restore onto an existing workspace, restore into new volumes, then boot a second Linux deployment. Profile, messages, threads, routines and file bytes match; the original access key and session still work and the restored model vault decrypts the saved credential.

The first hosting attempt failed because the controlled provider inherited the application image's health check; its fixture now disables that check. Relay now completes the empty-Ollama setup path through an explicit inline download, current-file byte progress, cancellation, reload recovery and a separate Use this model action. Downloads use the fixed loopback Ollama API; registry URLs, filesystem paths, caller credentials, insecure mode and recognized cloud tags are not accepted. The server verifies both a success event and the installed catalog. Owner authentication/same-origin writes, bounded streams, stale cancellation, request deduplication, shutdown/crash interruption and generic errors are exercised by seven new Node cases. The final 642-case suite passes with zero skips/failures.

Desktop and 390-pixel phone journeys pass against an independent streaming HTTP service: empty setup, progress, no popup, navigation/reload without duplicate pulls, cancel/retry, rejected model errors, custom-tag validation, installed-model preselection, explicit activation, actual assistant-worker conversation and a second download preserving the current connection. Both themes pass automated accessibility and horizontal-overflow checks. The native iPhone Simulator passes protected HTTPS setup, native keyboard/draft retention, a complete app restart during the download, cancellation/retry and actual worker conversation after activation. Native evidence uses a controlled model/registry transport; it does not claim physical-device or live-provider validation.

A real fresh-cache `smollm2:135m` download succeeded (270,898,672 installed bytes), then its live assistant request failed with HTTP 400 because this text-only model lacks tool support. The failure is retained in `work/local-model-download-live.log`; installation alone is not a tool-compatibility certificate. Capability-aware text-only chat remains an open product gap. Model download tests must use a tool-capable model for the full assistant journey. A second fresh-cache download installed `qwen3.5:0.8b` (1,244,131,636 bytes), but its assistant chose a clarification instead of the requested exact reply; that quality failure is retained in `work/local-model-download-live-qwen.log`. Neither small-model journey is counted as a passed assistant acceptance.

The suggested `qwen3.5:4b` passes the complete real Ollama registry-pull, explicit-selection and actual worker-reply journey, reusing previously installed test-cache weights. The rebuilt unsigned Mac app passes the same flow through its own bundled backend, then drains its owned Ollama on full quit and preserves the draft/download receipt on relaunch. A separate packaged Mac client also passes the full streaming-fixture UI journey. The final app archive byte-matches all 181 runtime files; saved iPhone Simulator and both mobile source bundles match all 47 production web assets. No test-server configuration is included. Completed receipts live in the sibling `relay-evidence/local-model-download-*-acceptance.json` files; the aggregate is `local-model-download-verification.json`.

The initial UI harness tried to reopen local setup after switching Settings categories, but that screen correctly retained its already-open setup. The test now verifies the retained progress directly. Earlier native captures predate a copy-only cleanup removing duplicate empty-catalog text; final production assets are built and synchronized separately. The implementation is original and uses the official Ollama API/model documentation linked in MODELS.md. Model weights stay in explicit test caches outside the source and release packages. Android runtime, physical devices, installing Ollama, arbitrary custom registries and broad model-quality acceptance remain open.

# Verification record

## Private browser in its own container — October 13, 2026

The production opt-in browser now runs in a separate non-root, read-only, network-none container, with no workspace/display/host mounts or debugging port. Its existing Computer page, owner sign-in handoff, exact reviews, file results and chat drafts remain the same. The original Node/Docker bridges carry Chromium’s CDP over inherited pipes; the existing HTTPS broker is its only implemented request path. The prepared image is pinned by digest, and policy is checked before and after attachment. This improves the browser process boundary while the feature remains experimental/off pending broader credential/taint and account/device acceptance. See TRUSTED_BROWSER.md.

The final Node suite and focused validation results are recorded in `browser-container-verification.json`. Six new cases cover rejected network/mount/privilege/resource changes, immutable image selection, minimal host environment and private helper permissions, shared launch, pre/post-attachment validation, redacted failure, close during startup, reconnect, local-engine restriction and lost-create-acknowledgement cleanup. The focused browser/container/API suite passed 18/18 before the final acknowledgement case was added.

Ten real Docker/Colima checks compose owner sign-in and assistant tool calls with the actual command freezer. They verify no private-browser host/workspace/display/socket access, distinct PID/network/mount namespaces, no public/VM-host/metadata TCP or UDP route, read-only root, enforced tmpfs capacity, bounded process configuration, exact reviewed form submission once, hidden credential observations, and independent browser cleanup preserving the desktop and files. No provider or owner account is used. Independent lifecycle acceptance kills a live container and its parent server process: a fresh browser can open, and parent SIGKILL removes the owned container and temporary helper.

This test exposed a real shutdown bug in the initial bridge: a file-stream read on a pipe could leave its host adapter alive after Chromium exited. The final bridge uses nonblocking pipe sockets. The repeated lifecycle test completes and cleans up. An earlier runtime check tried to inspect a deliberately frozen command container; its namespace observation was moved before takeover, while the actual freeze assertion remains. These were separate issues.

Desktop and 390-pixel phone-browser journeys pass real container rendering, username/password sign-in, blur-before-submit, one reviewed form write, reload during review, page-resource scope and exact-only fallback, decline, takeover, handback visibility, clear/cancel, preserved drafts/history, light/dark Axe checks and viewport fit. Both also pass exact website-download bytes, durable file associations, result cards, redirect review, declined dispatch and takeover cancellation. The first UI run had a five-second assertion while the cold container was still opening; its test wait now covers the container’s bounded launch timeout. Product errors are not bypassed and the final journeys pass.

Native iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Simulator passes actual protected-HTTPS WebKit, native keyboard sign-in, real Docker freezing during owner control, full app relaunch with a pending request, one page-load approval followed by a separate form review, exact submission once, handback, preserved draft/history and explicit private-data clearing. Result: `work/browser-container-native-1791824491538.xcresult`. This uses the actual separate Chromium container, a controlled model and an independent HTTPS account fixture.

Receipts and screenshots are in the sibling `relay-evidence` folder under `browser-container-*` and `container-browser-download*`. The final aggregate also records the rebuilt Mac package’s own backend, source/package checks and owner-preview state. Chromium’s nested sandbox is disabled inside the restricted container; the checks do not establish resistance to arbitrary browser/kernel/Docker exploits, general taint protection, every hosted login, payment handling, physical mobile devices or Android runtime. The full product goal stays active.

## Start installed Ollama inside Relay — October 12, 2026

**AI models → Use a local model → Start Ollama → Use this model** now removes the terminal step for an installed local runtime and model. Progress, cancellation and failure remain inline. **Start Ollama with Relay** is an explicit saved choice; startup options collapse after success. Changing the next-launch preference leaves current inference running. Startup neither selects a model nor adds chat messages. Installation and model downloads remain separate. See MODELS.md.

**629/629 Node tests pass**, with four-way concurrency and zero skips. Seven new cases cover the installed-executable boundary, a fixed `serve` command and sanitized environment, existing-service ownership, occupied ports, cancellation/deadline/early exit, durable private preferences and stale edits, bounded loopback health responses, owner/origin enforcement and real subprocess cleanup. The lifecycle case includes a group leader exiting while its child ignores termination, and a parent killed with SIGKILL; both descendants drain. Undefined/null startup inputs are rejected without launching a process. The earlier focused startup/discovery run passed 11/11; the final full suite covers the strengthened lifecycle case.

Desktop and 390-pixel phone-browser acceptance used actual installed Ollama 0.40.3 and `qwen3.5:4b` from a private existing cache on standard port 11434. They pass explicit startup, actual process cancellation, model selection, a real worker reply, retained drafts, no setup messages, light/dark Axe checks, viewport fit and cleanup. The unsigned packaged Mac app passes through its own bundled backend, including full quit stopping owned Ollama and full relaunch honoring the saved startup preference. Observed startup-to-catalog times were 312 ms desktop, 309 ms phone browser and 822 ms Mac in this run; these are observations, not latency guarantees. The first UI attempt caught a checkbox waiting for the server before reflecting a click; it now updates immediately and rolls back on failure.

Native iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Simulator passes protected HTTPS WebKit startup, explicit activation, real local inference, native keyboard entry and draft/reply preservation after full app relaunch. An independently launched Ollama app began using port 11434 before this test. The harness refused to disturb it, then relocated only its own real Ollama process and test HTTP transport to port 41134. The native receipt records that isolation. Production targets remain fixed, and standard-port native Mac/browser acceptance above is separate. The iPhone result is `work/local-model-start-native-1791818314298.xcresult`.

Evidence: `local-model-start-ui-acceptance.json`, `local-model-start-native-mac.json`, `local-model-start-native-ios-acceptance.json`, screenshots and the final `local-model-start-verification.json` in the sibling `relay-evidence` folder. Linux paths are implemented but lack runtime acceptance; Windows startup, other runtime launchers, automatic installation/downloads, hosted-provider certification, physical mobile devices, Android runtime and signed distribution remain open. The broader product goal remains active.

## Connect another device with one link — October 12, 2026

Data & devices now creates a five-minute, single-use connection link for a protected HTTPS Relay server. Open it in a browser or paste it into the native app’s existing connection dialog. The receiver reviews the server and full workspace access before connecting; no owner-key copying is required. The owner can cancel a pending link or disconnect a linked device. Revocation stays visible even if link creation is later disabled. These controls stay on their own page and do not add setup messages to chat. The iPhone server field now has a native clear button for replacing a saved address.

The independently implemented backend stores only grant hashes, binds pending links to the public origin, owner-key configuration and issuing session, atomically consumes each link, rate-limits failed exchanges and issues a separate seven-day Secure HttpOnly cookie. Pending grants stop working when their issuer expires or is revoked. Another device cannot replay a consumed link. Disconnect closes that device’s active streams immediately. Native preferences retain only the server origin; pending link tokens are absent from workspace state and browser storage.

**610/610 Node tests pass**, with zero skips, using concurrency four. Seven focused device-link cases also pass after the final disabled-creation revocation assertion. An earlier unconstrained run timed out in two existing timing-sensitive tests under host load; both passed unchanged in a focused recheck, followed by the complete passing bounded run. Production build, scoped formatting and whitespace checks pass. Android’s standalone Java suite passes **23/23** tests, including native link parsing; this is not Android app runtime acceptance.

Desktop and 390 × 844 phone-browser journeys pass creation/copy, cancel/expiry/reuse rejection, explicit connection review, secure sessions, real controlled-model replies, synchronized history with independent unfinished drafts, reload and immediate revocation. Axe checks, viewport fit and page-error checks pass. The separate desktop/phone session-recovery journeys also pass offline drafts, attachments, wrong-key feedback, reauthentication, synchronization and revoked streams.

The packaged unsigned Mac app passes its native connection window, cookie/origin persistence, full quit/relaunch, actual controlled-model conversation, preserved draft and owner revocation. Testing exposed a real startup race: Reload before the initial load completed opened a fatal dialog for the replaced navigation. The app now distinguishes that cancellation from startup failure. The final unattended regression deliberately holds the first stylesheet, reloads during initial loading and checks that the workspace opens with no fatal dialog. Holding the entire first document in an earlier test prevented Playwright’s initial window observation; the final harness holds only the stylesheet.

Native iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 acceptance passes actual HTTPS WebKit exchange, native keyboard entry, model reply, full app restart, owner revocation and a fresh link restoring the draft. The Swift parser case also passes. Results: `work/device-links-native-1791808430464.xcresult` and its `-parser.xcresult`; four captures are in `device-links-native-attachments`. Earlier attempts had a Simulator boot timeout and test text-replacement errors; the final test uses the visible native clear control and distinguishes placeholder text from entered values. Test roots are scoped to disposable Simulators, which were removed; the owner Simulator and host trust store are preserved.

The plain iOS Simulator build passes. The saved Mac archive matches **174 runtime files** and production metadata; the saved iOS app and synchronized iOS/Android source projects match **47 production web files** and contain no fixture server configuration. Evidence is collected in `device-links-verification.json`, individual acceptance/package receipts, screenshots and private logs. The owner server was backed up and refreshed with all state except revision preserved: nine messages, two files and owner computer control. Its local preview correctly reports that HTTPS and an owner key are required; no public exposure or access grant was created. Colima remains stopped.

This increment requires an already reachable protected HTTPS server. It does not provision hosting or tunnels, add team roles, establish live-provider quality, validate physical devices or Android runtime, or provide signed distribution. The broader product-parity goal remains open.

## Native iPhone restore points and handback race fix — October 11, 2026

The iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Simulator now passes the complete restore-point flow against an isolated HTTPS backend and real Docker computer: native keyboard/file creation, save, inline restore review and cancellation, relaunch during review, exact earlier-file restoration with later-only files absent, another relaunch, undo through the automatic before-restore point, removal review/cancellation/application, explicit handback, and return to the original draft with no new chat messages. Tests verify authoritative names, app/role/revision checks, literal email/mention boundaries, independent next drafts, group recipients, scoped real HTTP MCP calls, reviewed write/one continuation, inherited question/handoff/computer context, and HTTP rejection without unwanted messages. The production build and focused formatting checks pass; the existing Vite large-chunk warning remains.

Desktop and phone browser journeys and the final packaged Mac client pass the same `@` picker workflow. They cover keyboard/touch choices, cursor insertion with the remaining sentence intact, empty results/Escape, role restrictions, mixed file/app chips and file preview, per-chat draft navigation/reload, changed app revisions, exact action review, and a different selected app in the next draft. The confirmed continuation retains the original selected app. Axe, chip geometry, control separation and viewport fit pass. The native iPhone HTTPS journey passes actual WebKit keyboard/picker input, two full relaunches, separate Files navigation, one exact reviewed MCP write and retention of the next draft. Xcode result: `work/app-mentions-native-1791702097042.xcresult`; receipt `app-mentions-native-ios-acceptance.json`; captures in `app-mentions-native-attachments`. An earlier visual pass revealed a chip occupying an icon column and overlapping controls; the corrected composer layout has explicit geometry assertions, and the native rerun passes. The final app was subsequently rebuilt with the final empty-result accessibility label and web assets. Physical phones, Android runtime and live account/model behavior are separate gates.

Group browser regressions and both controlled mobile-share bridge journeys pass. Fourteen desktop/phone file-intake and actual Docker transfer checks pass after using the existing Relay Colima socket; the initial attempt used the default unavailable Docker socket. These tests also exercise the rearranged attachment area. Receipts: `groups-ui-acceptance.json`, `incoming-share-ui-acceptance.json`, `file-intake-ui-acceptance.json`.

The Mac archive matches 132 current server/shared/desktop/web files and production package metadata. The saved iOS Simulator app and synchronized iOS/Android projects match all 46 web assets; no test endpoint is embedded. Both disposable test Simulators are removed and the saved user Simulator remains. The owner preview was backed up and gracefully refreshed, preserving its nine messages, two files, complete workspace content, runtime configuration and stopped computer under owner control. Receipts: `app-mentions-mac-package.json`, `app-mentions-mobile-package.json`, `app-mentions-owner-refresh.json`; aggregate `app-mentions-verification.json`. App selection scopes connected MCP tools and does not add permission, approve actions, or constrain built-in tools. Full product parity remains open.

Desktop and phone browser journeys upload all three formats, read previews, download exact original bytes, invoke an independent HTTP model through the production agent/worker, and open revised Office results. Packaged Mac remote-client journeys pass too. Each surface uses nine HTTP model requests to read three inputs, create three results and return three replies; these are controlled provider responses, not live-model quality claims.

A separate packaged-local-Mac journey verifies all three parser Workers actually load from the app's bundled local backend, retaining attachments and drafts on reload. Native iPhone WebKit tests use a disposable trusted-HTTPS fixture and verify three staged Office attachment previews, nine HTTP model requests, new result pages, unchanged original bytes, revised content, and draft persistence after relaunch. Sources are staged by the test, not chosen through an OS file picker. ## Copy between your device and the private computer — October 11, 2026

**429/429 Node tests pass**, including six new clipboard cases and four input-queue regressions. Production TypeScript/Vite build, changed-file formatting, guest Python syntax and whitespace checks pass. **Ten real-Docker desktop/phone browser checks, three packaged Mac clipboard checks and one native iPhone Simulator XTest pass.** The existing nine direct-input/drag checks also pass against the rebuilt desktop image.

The browser/Mac journeys verify exact multiline Unicode, Copy/Cut/Paste ordered after fast typing, inline clipboard-denial recovery, no duplicate guest action on retry, no late overwrite after navigation, preserved drafts, no chat messages or clipboard contents in operation receipts, authenticated owner control, accessibility and viewport fit. An actual xterm test proves Copy reads its selection without interrupting the command, Cut is refused, and Ctrl-C still interrupts normally. The copy-size guard checks UTF-8 byte length; failed transfer is never automatically replayed.

The native test uses the current bundled iPhone WebKit, a dedicated HTTPS Relay server and a real isolated Docker desktop. Copy puts `Hello 你好 café 🌱` onto the iOS clipboard; the actual system Paste menu appends it to the existing draft without sending. The test prepares the guest selection through the owner API; native touch selection is covered by the earlier direct-input tests. This is Simulator evidence, not physical phones or Android runtime. The initial native failure came from a keyboard-position race in the test; waiting for the composer above the keyboard fixed it. A test exposed a real repeated-click hang in `xdotool mousemove --sync`; no-op moves no longer wait for a motion event, and the real input regressions pass.

The final Mac package also passes all six bundled-backend lifecycle checks, including background work with windows closed and full restart. All **132 packaged Mac runtime and Docker source files** match the current source/build. All **44 web assets** match both mobile projects and the saved iOS Simulator app. Its executable matches the tested Xcode app and embeds no fixture server URL. The native test Simulators were removed and the owner's saved Simulator was preserved. The owner server was backed up and refreshed with its nine messages, two files and computer control unchanged; its computer remains stopped. Android sync alone is not runtime acceptance.

Evidence: `../relay-evidence/computer-clipboard-ui-acceptance.json`, `computer-clipboard-native-mac-acceptance.json`, `computer-clipboard-native-ios-acceptance.json`, `clipboard-package-verification.json`, `clipboard-owner-refresh.json`, `clipboard-verification.json`, `computer-clipboard-phone.png` and `computer-clipboard-ios-pasted.png`. **Twelve tablet browser checks and three native iPad Simulator XTests pass.** The browser run exercises the production UI at 1180 × 820 and 820 × 1180 with touch enabled: photo decoding, original downloads, preview focus, removal, independent drafts, reload, returned images, actual image bytes in the HTTP model request, damaged-file fallback, incoming shares, light/dark accessibility and viewport fit.

The native run uses an iPad Air 11-inch (M4) Simulator on iOS 26.5 with actual WebKit, touch gestures, native keyboard and OS Paste. Seven semantic checks cover the real isolated Docker desktop: Take control freezes command execution, typed text arrives exactly, dragging moves the guest window, full app relaunch preserves the same desktop and unsaved work, and one-tap handback from Chat resumes agent control without adding messages or losing the Main draft. Search, focused Settings, Main/side drafts and photo preview also pass. The suite starts in landscape and rotates to portrait; the pasted image and caption survive full relaunch. Native screenshots confirm the portrait desktop and retained attachment.

The first run failed because the native test tapped the Settings row while it was below the landscape viewport. The harness now scrolls the actual row into view before tapping. Its earlier failure also prevented saving the test server, causing the dependent paste-relaunch failure. The corrected three-test run passes with no production-code change. The screenshot of the first image paste includes the Simulator's keyboard tutorial; the unobstructed portrait screenshot after relaunch confirms the retained thumbnail and caption.

Run `npm run photos:test:tablet` and, after building/syncing iOS and preparing the Docker computer, `npm run computer:test:direct-ipad`. The latter creates and deletes only its own Simulator and Docker resources. The previously saved Simulator is preserved. Existing universal iOS and Android web bundles still match all 44 current build files; no native distribution package was rebuilt for this acceptance-only pass.

Evidence: `../relay-evidence/tablet-verification.json`, `photo-chat-tablet-acceptance.json`, `computer-direct-native-ipad-acceptance.json`, and `tablet-native-attachments/`. This establishes Simulator and browser behavior, not a physical iPad, multitasking/window resizing, external keyboard, Apple Pencil, live provider judgment, Android runtime or complete product parity.

## Visible photo attachments — October 11, 2026

Photos now appear directly in the composer, incoming-share receipts, sent messages and assistant file cards. Draft thumbnails use a horizontal strip; multi-photo messages use a compact grid.
