Removing a draft attachment keeps its file. Malformed images show an explanation while the original remains downloadable; SVG/HTML is not embedded as a thumbnail. Thumbnail blob URLs are released on unmount. Clean attachment drafts no longer issue unnecessary saves on unmount.

**Three native iPhone Simulator XTests pass against the final build, with seven semantic checks.** Actual OS image paste produces a visible thumbnail; native tapping opens the full-image viewer and Save file control, closing preserves the caption, and a full app relaunch retains the attachment. The screenshot's green test image was inspected. The fixture source writes only to its disposable Simulator pasteboard. Computer input/dragging/handback and focused navigation regressions pass in the same run. All 44 web files match the saved Simulator app and packaged Mac; iOS/Android source bundles match too. No test endpoint is embedded. The test Simulator was deleted and the existing saved Simulator preserved. The owner web preview was refreshed without restarting the server or adding test data.

## Direct file intake — October 11, 2026

Drop files into Chat or Files, or paste an image into the composer. Chat retains attachments until Send. Uploads completing after navigation attach to the original conversation; count/size errors are checked before uploading, and a failed batch retains completed files without retrying. Direct device-file uploads and drops onto the owner-controlled computer copy into its current folder with conflict-safe names. Large text previews are bounded while downloads and computer transfers retain the original bytes; editing a shortened preview is disabled.

**407/407 Node tests pass**. The production build, changed-file formatting and whitespace checks pass. **14 desktop/phone browser file checks and seven packaged Mac checks pass**, using real HTTP/SQLite, a controlled local model and isolated actual Docker computers. They cover directory rejection, unhandled-drop navigation protection, exact bytes, unchanged plain-text paste, cross-chat upload completion, eight-file/size limits, attachment-only Send, partial failures, same-name preservation, return-to-Relay, accessibility and viewport fit. Drag/paste events are dispatched through the DOM and file selection uses the rendered input; this does not certify Finder gestures or the OS file chooser. An additional 22 draft/file/navigation browser regressions and four file/PDF regressions pass.

**Three native iPhone Simulator XTests pass with seven semantic checks**. Actual iOS Paste receives a PNG from a foreground debug-only source control through the OS pasteboard and native menu; production WebKit/upload/draft code retains the exact bytes and caption across full app relaunch without sending. The fixture source is excluded from Release builds and requires an explicit test argument. The initial test tried writing the Simulator clipboard from its background test runner and failed to find Paste; it was corrected to use the foreground source, not a synthetic paste event. Native keyboard entry, real Docker window dragging, owner handback, side chats, focused Settings and draft preservation also pass. This is Simulator acceptance, not a physical iPhone or a cross-app paste-permission certification.

The Mac arm64 app is rebuilt; 50 packaged web/runtime files match current sources. iOS and Android each contain 44 current synchronized web files. The passing iOS Simulator app was saved without a test server endpoint. The disposable Simulator was removed and the existing saved Simulator preserved. Android application/runtime acceptance, physical devices, live model/account acceptance, signed distribution and full product parity remain open.

The owner preview was refreshed after an online database backup. Its nine messages, two files, stopped computer and owner control were retained; only the startup revision changed. No test uploads were added there. Screenshots were inspected for the desktop drop target, phone computer files and native iPhone paste.

Two additional browser journeys verify explicit ordered backups, 429/503 recovery, restart-safe policies, one labeled reply, disable/remove and no setup history. Backend tests also cover no fallback after partial text/refusal, per-destination credentials, native reasoning boundaries, cancellation, owner takeover and no repeated completed tools.

The same finder/attachment flow passes in the existing packaged Mac remote client against the current isolated server. The first harness attempt timed out after forcing another navigation during native startup; the passing run uses the app's initial navigation. The file input is driven through the renderer, so this is not an operating-system chooser check. No native bundle was rebuilt.

These runs use real Relay/SQLite/worker/HTTP with controlled providers. This pass does not certify external accounts, rebuild native release packages or complete Android acceptance. The running owner computer session is preserved. The broader product goal remains open. Earlier counts below describe their recorded passes.

The latest browser runtime has **14 real-Chromium and owner-HTTP acceptance checks**. They cover HTTPS sign-in, encrypted session restoration, private accessibility observations, each redirect's separate review, stale-target rejection, exact binary/form bytes, keyboard submission, scrolling, known-secret rejection, takeover, durable receipts, authenticated owner endpoints and complete browser-data clearing. The earlier six-check authority harness also passes as a core regression. The production server's opt-in is exercised with the real computer authority; it rejects forged actors before launching a browser or guest. The full backend suite now passes **368/368**, including **26 vault/request tests, nine page-loading cases, six runtime/API tests and six assistant-integration tests**. The full result is recorded below.

The implementation is **experimental and off by default**. The host Chromium runtime, authenticated owner API, Computer Browser view and assistant tools are implemented. Seamless form-preserving handoff, complete host Chromium OS egress/resource auditing, a practical bounded review policy, wider credential handling and physical-device/Android/live-account acceptance remain. The acceptance used a controlled ownership contract and temporary HTTPS services, not the owner's workspace or browser profile. No native release packages were refreshed in this pass; the iPhone XCTest build below tests its remote-client path against current assets. W05 and the complete Muse/Grok/Dot goal remain open.

The runtime work exposed a redirect gap in the earlier Playwright routing harness; the redirected navigation reached the deny-all proxy and sign-in failed. Request-stage CDP interception now catches and independently reviews each hop. Additional regressions cover a review's permanent masking after credentials are forgotten, in-flight DNS admission limits, and authenticated API control checks. Production build and formatting pass. The core vault/request cases and runtime/API cases are distinct from browser acceptance and native-client coverage.

Desktop and 390px phone production-UI journeys now pass sign-in through accessible field overlays, committing a focused password before Submit, accurate post-login addresses, one reviewed form submission, review after reload, decline, takeover while waiting, paused model dispatch, retained Main drafts/history, data clearing, light/dark accessibility and viewport fit. They use an actual Relay worker and HTTP model protocol with independent local services. Production computer ownership logic is exercised, with container inspect/pause/resume transport replaced in this UI fixture. Six new Node tests cover gated tools, server-owned action IDs, takeover observation rejection, cancellation, durable receipt recovery and non-blocking review status. See `../relay-evidence/private-browser-ui-acceptance.json` and `../relay-evidence/private-browser-integration-verification.json`.

The first UI run exposed a review deadlock because screenshot capture waited for a navigation whose request still needed approval. The page now receives status and pending reviews independently of screenshots. Visual review also found a stale address after form navigation; the address now follows the loaded page. Reviews show decoded form fields first and keep protocol headers collapsed. The same journey also passes in the existing packaged Mac client connected to the current isolated server, with a separate app-data directory. Its local bundled backend was not rebuilt. Eight desktop/phone Main, stop, page-navigation and accessibility regressions pass. No native release or source archive was rebuilt, and the owner's live browser flag and data were unchanged.

The platform follow-up now passes **six actual Docker/Colima boundary checks** composed with the production browser and assistant tools. It verifies offline network/private PID namespaces, named volumes only, detached double-forked command freezing throughout owner sign-in, cancellation of pending agent requests, browser absence from the guest display and CDP TCP, tested guest-to-host reachability rejection, same-process handback and a reviewed form sent once. This is scoped boundary acceptance, not a complete browser/host escape or egress audit.

An **actual iPhone 17 Pro Simulator on iOS 26.5** also passes HTTPS owner login, saving its server through the native dialog, native keyboard username/password input, actual Docker freezing during sign-in, handback, complete app restart during a pending review, readable form approval and single delivery, retained chat draft/history, and Keep/Clear browser data. The test creates and removes its own Simulator, containers, volumes and temporary trust. It uses a controlled HTTP model and fictional account. Public server deployment, physical devices, Android and hosted-model/account behavior remain unverified.

This run found a mobile handback button hidden by keyboard-driven scrolling. A sticky control bar fixes it; desktop/phone and packaged Mac regression journeys now include actual hit testing after scroll. Username fields display normal text to their owner while password fields remain concealed; both disable autocapitalization/autocorrection and stay private in model observations. **359/359 Node tests**, the production build and changed-file formatting pass. Evidence: `../relay-evidence/private-browser-platform-verification.json`, `private-browser-real-runtime-acceptance.json` and `private-browser-native-ios-acceptance.json`. Existing release archives and the owner's browser opt-in/data remain unchanged.

The page-loading follow-up adds **nine Node cases** and **six actual Chromium/HTTPS checks**. A single explicit main-document approval loads its same-origin script, stylesheet, image and declared icon. Cross-origin images, frame documents and Fetch requests are observed waiting before any bytes reach the fixture; form submission still waits and occurs once. Finishing the action or redirecting the document revokes the scope. Exact-only approval retains individual resource reviews. Tests also cover 64-request/30-second limits, pending-dispatch revocation, restart, typed consent, forged metadata and duplicate approvals. The current full backend run is **368/368**, with production build and changed-file formatting verified separately. See `../relay-evidence/browser-page-load-verification.json` and `browser-page-load-runtime-acceptance.json` for platform results and precise scope. Desktop/phone and the packaged Mac remote client pass both page-loading and exact-only choices, stopping an exact-only load before resource bytes leave, and suppressing redundant review cards during an authorized connection. An actual iPhone Simulator with real Docker passes saved-server relaunch during review, one approval for script/style/image/icon, form review and one submission, handback, and Keep/Clear sign-in data. The native test now waits for the exact enabled confirmation control and verifies both its dismissal and the empty vault; its earlier failed result remains in the work directory. This is explicit bounded permission, not a general safe-read or taint classifier; the runtime remains off by default.

The previous UI pass simplifies finding pages and controls. The full Node suite passes **321/321**, and the complete browser regression passes **89 checks with two intentional sample-mode skips**. Four new desktop/phone checks cover one-tap search, keyboard focus and dismissal, focused Settings categories, Back/Forward, reload, unsaved edits and unchanged conversation history. Model, voice and app-connection journeys still pass against their independent HTTP fixtures. Native iPhone Simulator search, category navigation, draft preservation and relaunch pass. ## App sign-in — October 11, 2026

Ten OAuth cases plus the existing thirteen connector cases pass. They cover browser/state/PKCE/resource binding, encrypted persistence, review before installation, shared refresh for concurrent calls, rotating credentials, provider revocation, process interruption, removal during refresh and uncertain write receipts. No provider action is repeated automatically.

Production desktop and phone browser journeys pass collapsed technical fields, decline/retry, reload during sign-in, a temporary polling outage, explicit tool permissions, an actual MCP read, owner/CORS boundaries, unchanged chat history and draft, accessibility and viewport fit. The packaged Mac app passes the same flow through its production IPC bridge into a separate Chrome session; OS launch is intercepted only by that test. All 123 Mac runtime files match the current server/shared/web/native/desktop/container source. Real vendor consent, production client registration, physical devices, Android native execution and signed releases remain open. See [APP_CONNECTIONS.md](APP_CONNECTIONS.md).

An actual iPhone Simulator passes authenticated HTTPS setup, opening Safari through the native bridge, browser-bound code exchange, returning to explicit tool review, connecting only after review and retaining the chat draft. Its test root certificate is trusted only inside a disposable Simulator, which is removed afterward. The test pauses the companion through its ordinary UI and dismisses Safari’s first-run presentation tip; neither changes production behavior.

The full suite exposed an older ideas test that assumed notifications were enabled by default. Its fixture now opts in explicitly and uses a fixed clock; the production opt-in behavior is unchanged.

Android SDK license acceptance, physical signing and distribution remain outstanding. A provider can ignore cancellation; the worker is bounded and off the UI thread but this does not certify a hard deadline for every source app. No native release bundle or owner preview was replaced in this pass.

## Native mobile results — October 10, 2026

**Five iPhone Simulator XCTest cases pass against the production Swift implementation.** They exercise an actual WKWebView: generated scripts respond to interaction; the opaque child cannot access parent DOM or local storage; cookie writes do not persist; no Capacitor/device bridge is present; the website store is nonpersistent. A control WebView reaches a local HTTP probe, while generated fetches, images, form submission, popup and frame-navigation attempts produce zero probe requests. Other cases cover unloading/restarting the preview, exact exported bytes, distinct staging paths, cleanup, filename traversal, malformed base64 and the 8 MB boundary.

**Two controlled mobile bridge journeys pass**, one for each iOS/Android branch. They verify exact preview source and download bytes, busy-state handling, successful save, cancellation, failure and retry, an inert static frame in the main app, and no new chat history. These tests simulate the bridge; they do not prove the system file picker. **Fifteen result-browser regressions, five packaged Mac result checks, and six voice-memo journeys pass again.** The Java-only Android content harness also passes, including API-24-compatible decoding through the platform adapter. Android SDK compilation and WebView/device/picker checks remain unverified.

The native test loop caught and fixed an inherited content-policy issue: the parent frame originally blocked the child’s inline scripts. The parent now allows inline scripts while all generated content remains escaped into its opaque, offline child. Two initial assertions also assumed Chromium behavior: WebKit returns an empty cookie string and rejects forms through the sandbox without emitting a form-action CSP event. The corrected tests assert the required effects, including zero network requests, rather than a browser-specific exception.

Three initial simulator runs compiled but hung before the test runner connected; a reboot did not resolve it. Using Xcode’s default DerivedData directory and ad-hoc simulator signing allowed the tests to run. The unsuccessful bootstrap logs and failing policy runs are retained separately. The final XCTest result reports five passes and no failures. Computer Use still could not find the Simulator window, so **the full iOS Files-picker save/cancel journey and native preview pixels are not verified**. This is also not a physical-device certification.

Receipts: `mobile-results-ios-xctest.json`, `mobile-results-ios-bundle.json`, `mobile-results-ui.json`, `mobile-results-java.txt`, and the dated logs in the evidence directory. Live-model planning, physical devices and process restoration after guest replacement remain unverified or unfinished.

## Background computer jobs — October 10, 2026

The latest full Node run passes **134 tests**, including **eight job cases** for duplicate-launch protection, thread scope, concurrency bounds, frozen continuation, SQLite reopen, lost acknowledgements/runtime identity, takeover observation races, cancellation/account changes, graceful shutdown and bounded history. Production web and iOS Simulator builds passed. The macOS package is refreshed from this revision; package hash receipts record the exact runtime and iOS asset counts. **Six real Linux/Docker checks** verify an independently running job, live logs, service/SQLite reconnection to the same process, cancellation while frozen without unfreezing the runtime, bounded logs and actual result files, exactly-once continuation, timeout and interrupted-without-replay after computer restart. The first real fixture incorrectly required “running” at the launch acknowledgement, where “starting” is valid; it now accepts startup and separately waits for real execution/output. The final rerun passes all six, including a second concurrent job that stays alive after the first is cancelled. A launch barrier records the supervisor identity before execution, preventing a delayed start from appearing missing and executing later.

**Four production desktop/mobile flows** verify the job card, live output, owner pause/handback, automatic continuation/export of actual file bytes, direct cancellation, dedicated activity, reload and draft persistence, no synthetic user message, no horizontal overflow, no page errors and zero automated accessibility findings. The model responses are controlled fixtures; the jobs, file bytes and process boundaries are real. Captures: `jobs-paused-mobile.png`, `jobs-completed-mobile.png` and their desktop equivalents. Expected poll interruption during takeover initially logged as an ordinary failure; it now uses the worker’s cancellation path. The final focused run passes 23 computer/job cases after that correction and the navigation-card simplification. Repeated UI runs exposed slow Docker metadata requests on a busy host; runtime and volume inspection now use one combined daemon query, retaining all isolation validation. The test separately waits for handback, then allows up to two minutes for automatic result delivery. Prior timeout logs are retained; this is functional acceptance, not a latency guarantee.

Graceful Relay shutdown now detaches active jobs while closing network connections; explicit **Stop computer** still kills them. Job records retain the latest 32 KB per output stream and the newest 64 finished results. Expired records retain identity tombstones so a retried operation cannot relaunch. A replaced container is an interrupted job, not a restored process. Interactive PTY/input is covered by the newer pass above. Deliberate session-escaping children and full trusted-browser isolation remain open. The iPhone Simulator journey below was exercised after the Mac unlocked. Earlier browser/native receipts below retain their stated scope.

### Actual iPhone Simulator journey

The installed iOS app (iPhone 17 Pro, iOS 26.5) sent a request through the native UI. A real Linux background job created `native-report.txt`; its automatic continuation delivered the file to the same chat. The controlled model fixture chooses the tools; this is not live-model planning acceptance. The unsent draft survived app reinstall/relaunch and computer-page navigation. The actual guest screenshot appeared in the separate Computer page; native Take control and Give back to Relay reached their confirmed states, and the guest file appeared in the file section. This native check found a real bug: exported text stored as bytes downloaded correctly but displayed an empty preview/editor because the view only read the optional text field. The view now decodes the stored bytes for display while preserving original download bytes. The rebuilt app visibly displayed the exact generated contents. Both desktop/mobile browser flows were rerun and now assert readable preview and editor contents; all four passed. Updating the live test server's assets temporarily invalidated a cached lazy-loaded chunk; an explicit reload and app reinstall recovered the current build. This is a Simulator check, not a physical-device or Android pass. Native desktop background-job interaction remains unverified for this exact package.

## Earlier feature passes and evidence

The inline-decision pass adds six Node checks and ten browser journeys (four device-local sample, six self-hosted), with accessibility, reload, custom answer, dismissal and explicit retry coverage. The final broad Node run passed 85/86; a busy-host process-start deadline expired before the server logged readiness. The process-start allowance is now 30 seconds, while exit and health assertions are unchanged. The focused HTTP/question run passed 11/11, giving passing evidence for all 86 checks. An earlier cancellation test incorrectly counted the existing welcome message; it now compares against the recorded pre-cancel count.

The first decision browser run passed 9/13 applicable journeys; four waited for a fallback button label while the rendered button displayed its saved filename. The selector now targets the attachment control. The next run passed all 13 applicable journeys; two HTTP-failure variants are intentionally skipped for device-local storage, which does not use an HTTP save. Prior reports are retained. Native question and task checks are recorded separately from browser sizing.

The inline-voice pass completed **80 Node tests**. Browser verification covers **73 unique journeys**: 26 desktop, 26 phone-size, seven server and 14 voice journeys. The first full run passed 67/72. It exposed unrelated background results changing the voice tests' last-message target, and older task-card accessibility problems. Voice tests now create isolated side chats and await the actual reply; task headings and repeated card groups were corrected. A first focused rerun passed 12/14: installing a controlled clock after recording had started mixed real and virtual timers, and a loaded-host accessibility scan exceeded the test time limit. The clock is now installed before recording; voice checks have a 90-second overall allowance. The next voice run passed 13/14; the remaining media-error assertion now explicitly requests playback instead of relying on optional browser metadata preloading. Original failure reports are retained alongside the final reruns.

Dictation uses a real MediaRecorder with Chromium's synthetic microphone. Mic starts inline; Done explicitly transcribes to the editable draft without sending. Tests cover edits during recording, manual send, denial and late permission responses, cancel and background cleanup, the two-minute cap, draft length limits and retry with retained audio. Five Node checks cover transport, authentication/origin restrictions, credential redaction, header/size/response bounds, redirects, timeouts, concurrency and native permission boundaries.

Five additional Node checks cover saved speech: exact model payload and transcript, SQLite restart/cache, authenticated and ranged downloads, explicit export, cancellation/source changes/stale run guards, bounded/redacted errors and model tool integration. Six browser memo journeys cover inline generation, play/pause/seek, exact transcript, download, reload, exclusive playback, background pause, unavailable audio, cancellation and explicit retry. A generated tone stands in for speech. These checks do not prove real speech quality or OS microphone behavior.

New checks keep the interactive character inside the header, ensure automatic/manual/reduced motion still work, reject overlapping header controls at 320 pixels, and verify that the Latest messages control appears only when reading older content. A live-server check receives a background reply without moving the reader’s position, then jumps to the newest reply on request.

The main-chat test covers separate drafts, reload into Main chat, accessible drawer navigation and side-chat archive/restore. Shortcuts exercise files, ideas, tasks and app connections without losing a draft. Routine journeys cover create, edit, invalid time zones, previews, test history, enable/pause, archive/restore and reload. A controlled browser clock verifies that a due reminder posts once and a paused reminder stays quiet.

A native regression exposed a startup fallback: when the server was slow, the desktop renderer could select local demo storage after 1.8 seconds. Native desktop startup now waits up to 15 seconds and shows a retry state on failure, never an unrelated sample workspace. A browser regression holds the first response beyond the former deadline, then checks unavailable-server retry. A native diagnostic was invalidated by repackaging during the run; that report is retained, and the final package is tested after packaging completes.

Native desktop lifecycle receipts are in the sibling `relay-evidence/native-desktop.json` and `relay-evidence/native-desktop-packaged.json` files. The source-app receipt, public-browser and WebMCP checks are retained from earlier runs, rather than presented as newly repeated checks. ## Visual comparison

The supplied Muse reference was compared with the latest Relay chat capture. The shared structure is a centered assistant identity, Main chat with optional side chats, message bubbles, a bottom composer and five chat shortcuts. Relay’s original character, type and colors differ. The duplicate companion lane above the composer has been removed, repeated message avatars are gone, short bubbles fit their content, and work cards use fewer nested borders and smaller gaps. The character remains interactive in the header. In the same 1024 × 768 conversation, measured transcript height increased from 376.5 to 506 pixels and the composer area dropped from 199 to 120 pixels. These measurements compare Relay before/after, not a numeric similarity score against Muse. Message metadata, review surfaces, original typography and colors still differ from the reference. Exact pixel identity is neither measured nor achieved. The separate `../../reference-study/muse-comparison.html` page includes the before/after tablet comparison, current phone controls and original Muse reference; competitor artwork stays outside product source.

A separate local simplicity board at `../../reference-study/native-simplicity/index.html` compares an actual Grok Bot screenshot, the supplied Muse reference, and Relay before/after dictation. With permissions/service configured, sending a dictated message decreased from five actions to three; recording uses no modal. Dot's host app was blocked by computer-use controls. Later Grok panel captures did not reliably refresh, so the board makes no screenshot claims for those states. Private competitor captures are excluded from the product tree and source archive.

## What the tests establish

- Chat drafts, selected conversations, rename/archive/restore, queued follow-ups, stop/resume, copy/reply, search jumps, and editable memories work through the actual interface. Memory source links lead to the originating message; forgetting removes the saved note and retains the conversation history.
- A form request creates an actual filled PDF. Tests inspect downloaded AcroForm field values, render the PDF canvas, and require a human review before a sample email appears in Sent. Another server journey changes the current file and verifies that a pending review still previews and downloads its original frozen attachment.
- CSV imports calculate integer-cent totals from imported rows. An invalid import leaves the earlier data intact. Goals, milestones, page-tracker baselines, transition alerts, pause, and task lifecycle states persist.
- Desktop (1440 × 950) and mobile (390 × 844) journeys check dedicated workspace pages and conversational results for overflow, console errors, and automated WCAG 2 A/AA findings. Light and dark appearances are exercised. Automated accessibility checks do not replace a complete assistive-technology audit.
- The original anime character walks autonomously, greets on click/Enter, can be dragged within its lane and moved with arrow keys, and preserves the message draft. Quiet mode survives reload. Reduced motion freezes travel and sprite animation while leaving manual greeting available. These behaviors are checked on both browser sizes; native iPhone first-launch inspection separately confirms the asset renders there.
- Native macOS checks prove that the local worker finishes after all application windows close, native menu commands reopen the correct interface, and a full app restart preserves memories, conversations and completed work. They cover both the source app and the packaged arm64 app.
- Five skill tests cover validation and stale edits; slash input and draft preservation; immutable queued versions; explicit routine version updates; real SQLite reopen; model-visible instructions, inputs and file IDs; and external-action review. A scheduled skill cannot rewrite the skill library or create another schedule. Browser journeys exercise reply-to-skill drafts, run dialogs, search, archive/restore, keyboard selection and pinned routine versions.
- Eight incoming-email tests cover literal filter validation, initial baseline with no inbox replay, exact large history IDs, duplicate prevention, pagination and SQLite restart, whole-page retry after a partial fetch failure, sent/spam/deleted exclusions, edits and pause/resume during an in-flight batch, expired history, bounded bursts, disconnect races, frozen source/instruction versions, and review-only external writes. Desktop and mobile UI journeys create and test rules, inspect source snapshots, preserve drafts, reject enabling without Google, and handle filters with no matching mail. These use controlled Gmail responses, not a live mailbox.
- Seven scheduler tests cover daily, weekly, interval and one-time schedules; invalid input; IANA time zones; daylight-saving gaps and repeated hours; instruction snapshots; catch-up and non-overlap; exact-result notification filtering; test/failure visibility; saved-result reveal; cancellation; and bounded run history. Real SQLite tests reopen state and complete reminders without a browser or model call. Scheduled assistant writes still require review, and scheduled runs cannot create further routines.
- Server journeys observe background results without a page driving execution. HTTP and worker tests cover real SQLite transactions and process restart, lease recovery, cancellation, stale-result rejection, sessions, origin/host checks, and owned file downloads.
- Controlled model endpoints verify fragmented UTF-8 streaming, Responses and Chat Completions tool calls, bounded tool execution, and encrypted reasoning handoff. Controlled Google responses verify OAuth state, scopes, token protection, MIME attachments, account-generation and ETag checks, and unknown external-write outcomes without automatic retry.
- Docker controller tests inspect command arguments and container validation. They check the required non-root, offline, read-only, bounded configuration and rejection of an existing container with weaker settings.

## Limits of this verification

No real model credentials or Google account were supplied. Incoming-email polling against an actual mailbox remains unverified; controlled history responses exercise the complete adapter, cursor and worker path. Hosted-provider inference, OAuth consent, sending Gmail, and writing Google Calendar remain installation checks. The October 12 local-model entry records bounded actual Ollama/Qwen inference; earlier controlled results do not establish live-provider quality. The controlled tests verify Relay's adapters, not third-party service availability or every compatible provider.

Docker is now available through a dedicated Colima VM. The private desktop image build, commands, graphical input, file bridge and persistence have real-runtime coverage as described above. The older optional terminal adapter has separate controlled-configuration coverage. The public browser smoke used a real browser and a real public page, but did not attempt authenticated browser workflows, which the current reader deliberately does not support.

The sample assistant is deterministic and is labeled in the interface. Its workflows exercise real local state, PDF bytes, and calculations; it does not establish open-ended AI quality.

The macOS development package is unsigned and not notarized; Windows, Linux and Intel macOS packages have not been tested. The iOS build is for Simulator, not a signed device or App Store distribution. Android has not been built. Physical HTTPS deployment and device networks, Android share targets, real microphone/native mobile voice verification, physical push delivery, signed Mac notifications and real-device checks remain. Live transcription quality, live voice calls, real speech-provider quality, separate team bots, packaged marketplace skills, event triggers beyond incoming Gmail, arbitrary connector loading and cloud VM parity are unfinished. Timed routines, direct reminders and incoming-email triggers are implemented; unchanged-result filtering compares exact text and cards rather than semantic meaning. See [PRODUCT_PARITY.md](PRODUCT_PARITY.md) for the full open checklist.

## Reproduce the checks

```sh
npm ci
npx playwright install chromium
npm run format:check
npm test
npm run build
npm run test:e2e
npm audit
npm run desktop:test
npm run desktop:test:voice
npm run computer:setup
npm run computer:test
npm run computer:test:web
npm run computer:test:web-ui
npm run computer:test:takeover
npm run computer:test:runtime
npm run computer:test:jobs
npm run computer:test:jobs-ui
node scripts/test-computer-ui.mjs
npm run desktop:package
RELAY_DESKTOP_EXECUTABLE='./release/desktop/Relay-darwin-arm64/Relay.app/Contents/MacOS/Relay' npm run desktop:test
```

Use `RELAY_TEST_BASE_PORT=5280` (or another free pair of ports) when the default test port is occupied. The mobile build instructions are in [README.md](../README.md). Native UI checks require an unlocked Mac. Earlier simulator interaction checks were completed while it was available; the later iPhone job/computer flow is recorded above. Building source does not replace interaction checks.

For the optional real-network browser check, use the included script. On macOS it detects installed Chrome; set `RELAY_BROWSER_CHANNEL` as needed on your host:

```sh
node scripts/test-live-browser.mjs
```

Playwright saves an HTML report under `playwright-report/`, structured results under `test-results/`, and visual evidence in `RELAY_EVIDENCE_DIR` (or the sibling `relay-evidence` directory by default). The included GitHub Actions workflow runs the reproducible checks on Node 24 and uploads browser evidence. It has not been run on a public GitHub repository as part of this local build.

## October 14, 2026 — live desktop grounding and concise task completion

A genuine local Qwen3.5:27b / Quick run against the isolated Linux desktop exposed a coordinate-unit defect. The model read randomized screen facts but clicked normalized positions as pixels. The original host adapter now gives detected local Qwen3.5/Qwen3-VL vision models an explicit 0–1000 contract, persists the responding connection’s units on each tool call and converts at desktop execution. Other providers retain their default pixel contract. Bounds, contradictory units, two-way model fallback and owner-control enforcement have focused coverage.

Completed work now shows the final answer and retained result cards, without concatenating every intermediate tool narration. Streaming replaces the current progress text. Question/review pauses retain the latest explanation, while full tool context remains in the private recovery journal. Tests verify result-card retention, ordinary questions, streaming, steering and no replay after final checkpoint recovery.

- **853 automated tests passed**; **14 server/desktop/phone-browser regressions passed**, covering inline decisions, reload, drafts, reading position and startup failure. Production TypeScript/Vite build passed with the existing chunk-size warning.
- `scripts/test-computer-live.mjs` uses an original Tk checklist, randomized destination/reference/item order, real screenshots, a real Ollama model, real desktop input and the actual exported file. It requires GUI checkbox/Save events, disallows command/job shortcuts, checks guest/exported/downloaded bytes and requires the saved chat to match the model’s final response exactly. The post-task flow checks separate Computer navigation, owner takeover and preserved draft/history.
- Evidence retains failures: `computer-live/27b-quick-1` hit the original coordinate mismatch; `27b-normalized-1` completed the model task in 223,601 ms and downloaded matching bytes, then hit an outdated test navigation selector. `27b-normalized-release` corrected an accidental neighboring-checkbox selection but hit its 300-second budget before Save. `27b-normalized-mac` completed the model task in 187,548 ms with exactly two toggles and one Save, then hit a browser-only download listener in the test. That listener was replaced with Electron’s actual download API and independently checked against exact downloaded bytes.
- **Full packaged Mac acceptance passed** in `computer-live/27b-normalized-mac-final`: model work completed in **211,616 ms** within an explicitly recorded 600-second budget. It read Vienna / MAPLE-36387, performed exactly the two requested toggles and one Save, exported through `computer_export`, and used no command/job shortcut. Guest, Relay and Electron-downloaded bytes match. Separate Computer navigation, real owner takeover, return/reload with the same draft and unchanged message/run counts all passed. The final bubble exactly matches the final model answer, with no intermediate narration; page errors were absent. The test VM had no host mounts or published ports and the desktop/command containers had direct networking disabled. This is one fully passing randomized scenario alongside the retained earlier failure evidence.
- Mac download acceptance selects a test destination through Electron; it does **not** automate or certify the operating-system save picker. The packaged Mac client connects to an isolated local test backend; byte comparisons separately check every packaged server/shared/client/native-desktop/docker runtime file. This increment changes the backend, not the mobile bundle.

This is bounded real-model evidence, not universal visual grounding, latency or model-quality certification. The randomized timeout remains a known reliability limit. Hosted models, physical mobile devices, Android acceptance and unrestricted website workflows need separate verification. The owner’s existing sample chat and stopped computer profile remain separate from these temporary tests.

## Portable computer backups and recovery — October 14, 2026

Original encrypted backup transfer now exports a saved computer point through an owner-authenticated streaming API/CLI and imports into a fresh private point. The ordinary inline Restore review can recover that point after the original workspace volume is lost. No chat messages are added. Saved file bytes, browser-profile marker, Unicode paths, executable permissions and symlinks are covered; open-process memory is excluded.

Completed acceptance:

- `computer-backups/acceptance.json`: real Docker/Colima capture; private encrypted output; authentication; existing-output refusal; wrong-password/corruption cleanup; deletion of only the fixture’s desktop, workspace, display and saved-point volumes; import from the external encrypted file; restore through Chrome’s existing page; exact recovered file/profile bytes; owner command freeze; intact chat draft; desktop/phone layout and Axe checks; and reconciliation of an actual unfinished import helper/volume from a persisted journal.
- `computer-backups-regression/computer-snapshots-ui-acceptance.json`: complete existing desktop and phone-layout save/review/cancel/reload/restore/undo/remove lifecycle against real Docker, including a failed capture, no unexpected dialogs, no chat changes, no overflow and no Axe violations.
- `computer-backups-regression/computer-snapshots-native-mac-acceptance.json`: rebuilt packaged Mac local backend save/restore, full quit/relaunch, undo, saved draft and owner control.
- Existing failed-start rollback, interrupted frozen capture and sparse-file size rejection pass again in `work/computer-backup-snapshots-runtime.log`.
- 26 focused format, tampering, path traversal, helper isolation, missing-original rollback and source-release boundary checks pass. An earlier full backend run passed 872/872; the final source verification also includes the subsequently added helper-isolation case. The aggregate `computer-backups-verification.json` records the final source revision, clean rebuild, final backend count and preview preservation.

Backup transfer currently uses the administrator CLI; native export/import pickers are not implemented. This simulates owned-volume loss, not physical disk failure or automatic replacement of a whole VM. Live browser sign-in compatibility, remote-engine migration, Android and physical-device backup flows remain unverified. The prior Linux installer is a separate earlier release; this increment rebuilds the Mac package and source archive.

Mac packaging initially failed twice because GitHub’s checksum download timed out. Packager now uses the checksums included in the pinned official Electron npm package, retaining runtime hash verification and allowing the verified cache to be reused. Backup files are excluded from Git, Docker contexts, desktop bundles and source-release allowlists. An early transfer test exposed a FileHandle stream-close hang; the CLI now uses explicitly owned descriptors, and its complete subprocess exit succeeds. Neither failed attempt is counted as acceptance.

See [Computer backups](COMPUTER_BACKUPS.md) for commands, encryption parameters, archive limits, metadata preservation and recovery prerequisites. There is no production model/account, payment, signed-store distribution or whole-product completion claim in this increment.

## In-app computer backup controls — October 14, 2026

Computer → Restore points now provides Save backup to a file and Import backup forms. Saving captures a point or uses an existing one; native apps stream the encrypted file to the system save picker. Browser downloads remain ordinary downloads. Import opens the existing inline Restore review before changing current files. Passwords, transfers and page navigation stay out of chat history.

The acceptance harnesses and aggregate `backup-controls-verification.json` record the exact build and completed checks:

- Desktop/phone browser acceptance uses real isolated Docker data and an encrypted file larger than 10 MB. Password mismatch prevents capture; wrong-password import retains the chosen file; successful import leaves current bytes unchanged until reviewed restore; exact original bytes and the chat draft survive. There are no added chat messages, unexpected browser dialogs, Axe violations or horizontal overflow.
- Packaged Mac acceptance uses the actual local backend, native bridge and filesystem. The test supplies cancel/save responses to the native dialog rather than automating the macOS Save panel. Cancel writes no destination; retry preserves the complete encrypted file; import/review/restore succeeds.
- The iPhone Simulator harness uses actual system save and import pickers, verifies cancellation cleanup, compares exported bytes, restores through review and checks the draft after full app relaunch. The Swift staging tests separately exercise 12 MiB data, order, size, headers and cleanup. Android's pure-Java staging helper passes on the private JDK 21; no Android SDK, plugin build or Android runtime acceptance is claimed.
- Session-bound HTTP tests reject another session's status/download/cancel requests, and successful browser download removes its ticket. Encrypted staging cleanup ignores current, unfamiliar and linked directories and tolerates concurrent cleanup. Closing the Mac window during a pending save abandons the staging without writing a destination.

Early iPhone harness attempts stopped at a duplicate WebKit accessibility label and a tap consumed while dismissing the password keyboard. These failures are retained alongside the corrected acceptance, not counted as passes. Full-size 8 GiB device saves, physical devices, remote-engine migration and live browser sign-in continuity remain outside this acceptance. The earlier Linux installer is not rebuilt in this increment. This continues the product goal; it does not complete it.

## Local memory reviews without extra chat steps — October 14, 2026

A current-build real-model rerun passed the seven-turn conversation journey with Qwen3.5:9b Quick, while 4b produced no usable memory proposal. Constraining 4b's numbered JSON proposals saved the first preference but incorrectly created a second conflicting note during correction, leading to an unnecessary question. These attempts remain under `memory-conversation/current-*` and `structured-4b`.

Local Ollama Quick review now uses constrained JSON proposals, with corrections naming the exact saved note text. Relay resolves that text to one frozen owned identity and retains the existing quote, ordering, cancellation, learning-off and owner-edit checks. Unknown/ambiguous references and unexpected fields are rejected. Normal reasoning and other providers retain the existing numbered tool/JSON path. Ordinary replies keep their normal model settings; there are no new screens, dialogs or transcript entries.

Seven new Node cases cover the actual local adapter wire, identity-preserving updates, protected and concurrent owner edits, invalid evidence/targets, explicit saves while learning is off, bounded language hints and current reply context. Integration checks ensure both tool-capable and chat-only reply prompts receive the actual saved result. The final clean-source backend suite contains 889 tests. `scripts/test-memory-learning-live.mjs` adds ten independent actual-model scenarios: English/Chinese preferences and corrections, retained details, protected wording, learning off, explicit saves, fictional/hypothetical text and instructions inside a saved note. It verifies current source quotes and isolation from another assistant. The first 9b semantic run saved a Chinese fact in English; the language instruction and schema description now explicitly preserve the evidence language. That failed receipt remains under `semantic-9b`. A later global Chinese-language instruction caused 4b to translate an English note into Chinese (`final-chat-4b`); it was replaced with a hint tied to recognized statement language. The broad hint also matched a statement inside a fictional quote in the 4b `hinted-semantic` run. Recognition is now anchored to statement boundaries, and the prompt explicitly says a language hint is not evidence to save. Final acceptance paths are identified in the aggregate receipt; all failed attempts are retained. Mixed or unknown language is not forced to English or Chinese.

Final live conversation and semantic receipts, clean source rebuild, packaged Mac regression, runtime-byte comparison and preview preservation are collected in `memory-conversation-verification.json`. The Mac test uses its actual packaged local backend/database and a controlled HTTP model; full quit/relaunch, retained draft, new-chat recall, correction, learning-off and explicit save/forget boundaries are exercised. Actual Qwen inference is separately tested through the phone browser on an isolated Ollama port/cache. The owner’s model configuration is unchanged. Frontend/mobile web assets are unchanged in this server increment; this does not add Android or physical-device acceptance.

A later 9b chat run correctly recalled “avoid meat and include mushrooms,” but the harness required the literal word “vegetarian.” Reply checks now also accept direct meat-free/no-meat/avoid-meat paraphrases while preserving the mushroom, storage, identity and no-extra-action checks. The earlier stopped run remains under `anchored-chat-9b`.

The `release-chat-4b` run saved its correction correctly but then asked permission to update it again. The `release-chat-9b` run removed all notes correctly but claimed preferences were still saved; the earlier test only rejected repeating the deleted facts, so its pass did not establish honest empty-state recall. The live test now also requires an explicit negative acknowledgment and rejects that false claim. Reply context is rebuilt from current assistant-owned records: completed saves are already done, protected proposals remain pending, and an empty list is explicitly empty. It does not reuse a stale save promise or suppress tools needed for a compound request. The `context-chat-4b` run still asked for save permission despite the receipt. Simple English preference statements whose entire content matches the saved evidence, optionally wrapped in a short remember instruction, now receive a host acknowledgment after the save or proposal succeeds. Compound requests, attachments, selected apps, answered questions, follow-up steering and unknown wording retain normal inference. A new regression checks exact-source acknowledgment, protected proposals, compound-request exclusions and durable replay. The original failed and insufficient-check receipts remain available. Final conversation paths use the stricter check.

These are bounded scenarios, not universal memory judgment or conversation-quality certification. A model can still misinterpret a preference or produce an overlong reply or optional offer. Earlier failed replies remain available; a schema guarantees shape, not meaning. No competitor code, external memory service or additional provider is used.
